Ef you figgers on gettin’ a big passel of these cullud folks ’round here to jine in with us, you’ll have t’ have some ’ficials with scrumptious names, and passwords and grips. Dese here ign’ant folks needs somethin’ like dat to catch their ’magination. If you put dat in, they’ll jine like flies ’round molasses.”

===Chinese===
其他种种细节，也都这样一一摆出来，加以讨论，或采纳、或修改。还得给它起个名字。肯尼思本想取一个简短朴素的，可在这件事上他被推翻了。要让它对大多数未来成员所属的那个质朴、不识字的阶层有吸引力，一个响亮、气派的名字是必要的。他们议定，叫「全国黑人农户合作保护同盟（The National Negro Farmers’ Cooperative and Protective League）」。

起初大家觉得这计划未免有点好高骛远，可肯尼思越是讲得投入，尽可能把话说得浅显而有力量，那些反对意见便一条一条地被化解了。不过，有一处非改不可。这意见是海勒姆·塔克提出来的。

「你就不打算弄点暗号、口令，还有什么握手礼，像那个什么奇异兄弟会啦、共济会啦、毕达哥拉斯骑士团[2]啦他们那一套？」他问。

「我没觉得有这个必要，」肯尼思答道。

「嘿，那我跟你说，孩子。你要是指望着把这一带一大帮子有色人招进来入伙，你就得弄几个名头唬人的职衔，还得有口令、有握手礼。这帮子不识字的人哪，就吃这一套，非得有这玩意儿才勾得住他们的心思。你把这写进去，他们入起会来，就跟苍蝇围着糖蜜转似的。」

===Original===
Kenneth had hoped that the society would be run on a dignified and intelligent basis, but he realized that Hiram Tucker might be right after all. Most of the sharecroppers were ignorant﻿—at least, illiterate. Mere show and pomp and colourful uniforms and high-sounding names played a large part in their lives, which, after all, wasn’t so much a racial as a human trait. Hadn’t the Ku Klux Klan outdone, in absurdity of name and ceremony and dress, anything that Negroes had ever even thought of?

This question was disposed of, after more discussion, by the adoption of Hiram Tucker’s suggestion. Kenneth was appointed to work out the details of organization, and the meeting adjourned. The National Negro Farmer’s Cooperative and Protective League had been born.

===Chinese===
肯尼思原本指望这个团体能在一个体面而讲理智的基础上运转，可他意识到，到底还是海勒姆·塔克说得对。那些佃农大多蒙昧无知——至少是不识字。光是排场、铺张、花花绿绿的制服和响亮的名头，在他们生活里就占了很大的分量，而这说到底，与其说是哪一族的特性，不如说是人的天性。那三K党在名号、仪式和衣着上的荒唐，岂不是黑人连想都想不到的吗？

这一问题，经过进一步讨论，因采纳海勒姆·塔克的建议而告解决。肯尼思被委派去把组织的各项细节拟订出来，会议便散了。全国黑人农户合作保护同盟（The National Negro Farmers’ Cooperative and Protective League）[3]就此诞生了。

---

> 译注：
>
> [1] **nigger（黑鬼）**：本章此处出自黑人老妇人阿莫斯太太之口（黑人口语 dat/nigger/hisself/po’ 等），系黑人在底层群体内部沿用白人种族主义贬语以讥刺同族中「发迹」者——作者怀特意在刻画种族压迫语境下底层内部的自我贬抑与攀比心理。译文保留「黑鬼（nigger）」之贬义冲击力不软化（与第1章以来全书处理一致），并据其口吻译乡土中文。下文三K党恐吓信中的「Nigger」为白人种族主义恐吓用语，照例保留冲击力。
>
> [2] **Odd Fellows / Masons / Knights of Pythias（奇异兄弟会 / 共济会 / 毕达哥拉斯骑士团）**：美国三大兄弟会组织，均以暗号、口令、握手礼（grip）及 elaborate 仪式著称。「Odd Fellows」（独立奇异兄弟会，Independent Order of Odd Fellows）、「Masons」（共济会，Freemasons）、「Knights of Pythias」（毕达哥拉斯骑士团，以古希腊哲学家毕达哥拉斯之谊为名）在十九世纪末至二十世纪初的美国社会生活中影响甚广，黑人间亦有相应的平行组织（如黑人的「黑白骑士团」Prince Hall Freemasonry）。海勒姆·塔克据此建议农户合作社也采用类似仪式化包装，以吸引不识字的农户。
>
> [3] **The National Negro Farmers’ Cooperative and Protective League（全国黑人农户合作保护同盟）**：本章农户合作社正式定名，旨在联合黑人农户集中采购物资（糖、面粉、鞋、衣物、肥料、种子、农具）、并以积累的辩护基金在法庭上为受东家欺瞒的佃农打试验性官司。首部分部设于阿什兰（呼应第1章阿什兰佃农与东家之争、第8章分成制弊端陈述），计划逐步铺开至全县。原文末段作「National Negro Farmer’s」（单数所有格），与前文「Farmers’」（复数所有格）不一致，显系排版之误，译文统一作「农户合作保护同盟」。

## XIII / 十三

===Original===
The days that followed were full of interest for Kenneth and Jane. The constitution and bylaws were drafted and approved and sent to Atlanta to be printed by a coloured printing firm. Judge Stevenson prepared the articles of incorporation and did the necessary legal work, still refusing any pay for his services. Kenneth had offered to pay him out of his own pocket, but the judge told him: “Keep your money, Ken, I c’n wait. I’m gettin’ along in years now and I’ve been hopin’ that this problem that’s cursin’ the South would be settled befo’ I passed on. But what with these damn fool Kluxers kickin’ up hell ’round here, I don’t know whether I’ll see it or not. Your idea may do some good—I don’t know whether it will or not—but if I c’n help, let me know.” Kenneth thanked him and had been immeasurably encouraged by the old man’s attitude.

===Chinese===
接下来的日子，对肯尼思和简来说，过得既充实又有意思。章程和附则起草出来、议定通过，送到亚特兰大，交一家有色人（coloured）印刷厂去排印。史蒂文森法官拟好了公司组织章程，把该办的法律手续一一办妥，仍旧不肯收一文报酬。肯尼思提出要自掏腰包付给他，老法官却对他说：「钱你留着吧，肯，我等得起。我年纪也大了，一直盼着这个祸害南方的问题能在我闭眼之前有个了结。可眼下这帮该死的蠢三K党徒在这一带胡作非为、闹得天翻地覆，我能不能活到那一天，我也说不准。你这个主意也许管点用——管不管用我也不知道——可要是帮得上忙的，你只管开口。」肯尼思向他道谢，老人这份态度给了他莫大的鼓舞。

===Original===
As soon as the literature they had ordered was received, the first meeting was called by Tucker and Tracy at Ashland. Jane and her father drove out with Kenneth, who was to present the plan to the group gathered. The meeting was held at a little wooden church, whitewashed on the outside, and furnished within only with rude benches. On the walls were one or two highly coloured lithographs of religious subjects. The hall seated not more than two hundred and was crowded to capacity. Even the windows were comfortably filled by those unable to obtain a seat on the floor. The illumination was furnished by four kerosene lamps attached to the walls, two on each side.

Hiram Tucker acted as chairman, while Tom Tracy took minutes of the meeting. After a preliminary announcement of the purpose of the gathering by the chairman, Kenneth was called upon to outline the plan that had been proposed. At the outset, having had no experience as a public speaker, he stumbled and faltered and knew not what to do with his hands. After a few minutes he jammed them into the side pockets of his coat and, warming to his subject, swung into a clear, forceful, and convincing recital of the purpose and possibilities of the cooperative societies. His enthusiasm became infectious. His audience began to share his zeal. Humble and lowly folks, their vision limited by the life they led, they had the feeling, as Kenneth talked on, of having been face to face with a blank wall of immeasurable height and impenetrable thickness. Under the spell of his words they seemed to see the miraculous opening of a door in this wall. Hope, which had been crushed to earth year after year by disappointing settlements for their labour, began to mount.

===Chinese===
他们订购的宣传资料一到手，塔克和特雷西就在阿什兰召集了第一次大会。简和她父亲开车陪肯尼思一道去，由肯尼思向到会的人宣讲拟好的计划。会议在一座小木教堂里开，外头刷了白灰，里头只有些粗陋的长凳。墙上挂着那么一两幅色彩浓艳的宗教石印画。这厅堂至多坐得下两百人，此时已挤得满满当当，连窗户上都舒舒服服地趴满了人——都是地板上抢不到座位的。屋里靠墙挂着的四盏煤油灯照明，两边各两盏。

海勒姆·塔克当主席，汤姆·特雷西做记录。主席先开场宣布了此次集会的宗旨，接着便请肯尼思把拟好的计划细细说来。一开始，他没有什么当众讲演的经验，磕磕巴巴，半天才吐出半句，两只手也不知往哪儿放才好。过了几分钟，他把手往外套两边口袋里一插，话一讲顺了，便滔滔不绝地讲起合作社的宗旨和种种可能，条理分明，有力量，有说服力。他这股热忱有了传染性，底下听的人也跟着一同热起来。这些卑微、低微的人，一辈子过的日子限住了他们的眼界，听肯尼思讲下去，他们原以为自己是面对一堵不可度量其高、也不可穿透其厚的高墙，已经走到尽头。在他的话的魔力下，他们仿佛看见这墙上奇迹般地打开了一扇门。一年又一年，那因结算劳作所得而屡屡落空、被狠狠踩进土里的希望，又开始悄悄抬头。

===Original===
As for Kenneth, he had forgotten his self-imposed inhibitions and prohibitions. Gone was the hesitation and doubt. He had seen a light where he had thought there was no light. His voice rang true and firm, and there was a look of eager earnestness on his face as the pale, flickering light from the oil lamps illumined it.

He finished with a flourish so dear to the hearts of coloured audiences. It was what the old-style coloured preacher used to call “de ’rousements.”

===Chinese===
至于肯尼思，他早已忘掉自己给自己设下的那些禁制与戒律。迟疑和动摇都一扫而空。他原以为没有光的地方，他已经看见了光。他的嗓音清亮而坚定，油灯那苍白、摇曳的光映在他脸上，他一脸急切而恳挚的神情。

他这一段结尾收得慷慨激昂——这正是有色人听众最听得入心的一种收尾。也就是那种老派有色人牧师常说的「鼓动劲儿（’rousements）」。

===Original===
“You husbands and sons and brothers, three years ago you were called on to fight for liberty and justice and democracy! Are you getting it?” He was answered by a rousing “No!” “What are you going to do about it?” he demanded. “Single-handed, you can do nothing! Organized, you can strike a blow for freedom, not only for yourselves but for countless generations of coloured children yet unborn! No race in all history has ever had its liberties and rights handed to it on a silver platter—such rights can come only when men are willing to struggle and sacrifice and work and die, if need be, to obtain them! I call on you here tonight to join in this movement which shall in time strike from our hands and feet the shackles which bind them, that we may move on as a race together to that greater freedom which we have so long desired and which so long has been denied us! Only slaves and cowards whine and beg! Men and women stand true and firm and struggle onwards and upwards until they reach their goal!” He paused impressively while the audience sat mute. He looked over the assemblage for a full minute and then demanded in a ringing voice: “What do you choose to be slaves or men?”

He sat down. A salvo of applause greeted him. A Daniel had arisen to lead them! Kenneth took on a new importance and affection in the eyes and minds of his hearers. He had heard their Macedonian cry and answered it.

===Chinese===
「你们这些做丈夫的、做儿子的、做兄弟的——三年前，人家叫你们去为自由、为正义、为民主打仗！如今你们得到这些了吗？」回答他的是一声炸雷般的「没有！」「那你们打算怎么办？」他追问。「单枪匹马，你们什么也办不成！组织起来，你们就能为自由奋力一击——不光是为你们自己，更是为千千万万尚未出生的有色人孩子！有史以来，没有一个种族的自由和权利是别人用银盘子端到它面前的——这样的权利，只有当人愿意去挣扎、去牺牲、去流汗、必要时去送命，才能到手！今晚我就在这儿号召你们，加入这一运动——它迟早要从我们手脚上砸断那捆绑着我们的镣铐，好让我们作为一个种族一同前行，走向我们渴慕已久、也被剥夺了已久的那更大的自由！只有奴隶和懦夫才哼哼唧唧、伸手乞讨！真正的男人和女人，他们立得稳、站得直，一路向前、向上，挣扎奋斗，直到达到目标！」他有意顿了一顿，全场鸦雀无声。他把全场扫视了足有一分钟，然后以清亮的嗓音发问：「你们要做什么——奴隶，还是人？」

他坐下了。一阵雷鸣般的掌声向他涌来。一位但以理[1]在他们中间兴起，要引领他们了！在听众的眼里、心里，肯尼思有了新的分量，也添了新的亲厚。他听见了他们马其顿人的呼声[2]，并作出了回应。

===Original===
As he mopped his brow, Kenneth felt that he had made a good beginning, although he was a bit ashamed of having made so direct an appeal at the end to emotion instead of to reason. At the same time he knew that it had been necessary. “’Rousements” were absolutely essential to awaken the response needed to get the cooperative societies under way. Without them his humble audience might not have been aroused to the point of action that was so necessary.

Following Kenneth, Mr. Wilson made a stirring appeal to the crowd to come forward and give their names if they wanted to join the newly formed society. Those who had the money were urged to join at once. At first, only a few came forward. Then they came in numbers until around the table at which sat Secretary Tracy there was an excited, chattering, milling throng.

===Chinese===
肯尼思抹了一把额头，觉得自己有了一个不错的开端，只是结尾处他如此直白地诉诸情感而非理智，心里多少有点惭愧。但他同时也明白，这是必须的。「鼓动劲儿」是绝对少不了的——非要这样才能激发起把合作社办起来所需的反响。没有这一手，他这些质朴谦卑的听众，也许就激发不出那股非有不可的行动劲儿。

肯尼思讲完，威尔逊先生接着登台，热烈地呼吁大家上前报名入会——凡是愿意加入这个新成立的团体的人都报上名来。手头有钱的，更被劝着当场就入会。起初，只有零星几个人走上前。接着，人一拨接一拨地涌上去，到最后，秘书特雷西坐的那张桌子四周，已围成一圈又兴奋、又嘈杂、拥来挤去的人海。

===Original===
After the meeting Mr. Phillips accepted Mr. Wilson’s invitation to ride home in his car. Kenneth did not object—it enabled him to be alone with Jane. They talked of the meeting as they walked to the car. Jane gave Kenneth’s hand a faint squeeze. “Oh, Kenneth, you were splendid!” she declared.

It was a perfect night—one created for making love. A soft light filtered through the leaves of the trees, casting a lace-like shadow on the earth. The air was soft and languorous, as it can be only on a spring evening in the South, as soft and caressing as the touch of a baby’s hands. From near at hand came the mingled odour of honeysuckle and cape jasmine and magnolia blossoms and roses. The world seemed at peace. No sound disturbed the air save the chattering and singing of a mockingbird, as lovely as the sob of velvety, full-throated violins, and the voices, growing fainter and fainter, of the crowd leaving the now deserted church. It would have taken a much stronger man than Kenneth to resist the spell of so perfect an evening. He was not mawkishly sentimental—rather he detested the moon-calfish type of man who rolled his eyes and whispered empty, silly compliments in the ear of whatever girl he met. On the other hand, he was amazingly ignorant of women. As a youngster he had been exceedingly chary of the little girls of the neighbourhood, preferring to spend his time playing baseball or shooting marbles. This shyness had never entirely left him. From his youth on he had had but one strong passion in his life that passion had possessed his every thought and in it was centred his every ambition—his desire and determination to become a great surgeon. His one serious venture into the realm of lovemaking had been the affair with the girl in New York, but that had not taken a strong enough hold upon him to leave much of a mark. So rapidly had it begun and ended that he had had in it little experience in the great American sport of “petting.” It was thus easy for him to fall head over heels in love with Jane, for she was, in fact, the first girl in his life outside of his sister who had come into his life in more than a casual way.

===Chinese===
会后，菲利普斯先生应了威尔逊先生的邀请，搭他的车回去。肯尼思并不反对——这倒让他能和简单独待在一起。他们一边朝汽车走，一边说着方才的会。简轻轻捏了捏肯尼思的手。「哦，肯尼思，你太棒了！」她说。

这是个完美的夜晚——一个天生为谈情说爱而造的夜晚。柔和的光透过树叶筛下来，在地上投下花边般的阴影。空气柔软而慵懒，只有南方春天的夜晚才能这样，柔软而爱抚，像婴儿的小手摸在脸上一样。近处飘来金银花、月橘、木兰和玫瑰混合在一起的香气。整个世界仿佛都已安睡。除了反舌鸟[3]婉转啼鸣——那声音之美，一如丝绒般嗓音饱满的小提琴的低泣——以及渐行渐远、正离开这座如今已空荡荡的教堂的人群的说话声，再没有什么惊扰这一片空气。要抵御这样一个完美夜晚的魔力，需要比肯尼思硬气得多的男人。他并非多愁善感到肉麻的那种人——相反，他讨厌那种痴情种子的男人，眼睛骨碌碌转，见着哪位姑娘都凑到人家耳边，低声说些空洞、愚蠢的奉承话。可在另一方面，他对女人简直惊人地无知。小时候，他极怕招惹邻里的小女孩，宁可把时间都花在打棒球、弹玻璃珠上。这份羞怯一辈子也没完全离开过他。从少年时起，他这一生只有过一种强烈的激情——这股激情占据了他每一念的心思，他一切的抱负都围绕着它——那就是成为一名伟大外科医生的渴望与决心。他唯一一次认真走进谈情说爱这一领域，是和纽约那个姑娘的一段情，可那段情并没在他身上扎下足够深的根，没能留下多深的印记。它来去都那么快，以至于他从中所得的，对于「亲昵（petting）」这门美国大运动而言，几乎谈不上什么经验。因此，他很容易就一头栽进对简的热恋里——因为事实上，除妹妹之外，简是他生命中第一个不只是萍水相逢走进来的姑娘。

===Original===
Jane, on the other hand, had, innocently enough, flirted as every pretty girl (and many who are not pretty) will do. She appreciated Kenneth’s fine qualities: he was capable, industrious, and handsome in a way. He annoyed her at times by his almost bovine stupidity in expressing his love. She naturally liked the idea of having the love of a man who is naive, who has not run the whole gamut of emotions in affairs with other girls; yet, also naturally enough, she did expect him to have at least some savoir faire, to be able to win her with some degree of the finesse that every girl wants and expects. She resented his businesslike matter-of-factness in seeking her—as coldly calculating, it seemed to her, as though he were operating on one of his patients. In this she was doing him an injustice. Underneath his surface placidity Kenneth’s love had become a raging flame—he cursed the shell of professional dignity which had crossed over and become a part of himself.

Thus they walked through the soft spring air, she wishing he would do that which he in his ignorance felt would be the unwisest thing he could attempt. Thus is life made up of paradoxical situations where a word, a look, an otherwise insignificant gesture, would clear away at one fell swoop mountainous clouds of doubt and misunderstanding.

===Chinese===
简呢，在另一方面，则像每一个漂亮姑娘（许多不算漂亮的也一样）那样，多少也算清白无罪地调过情。她欣赏肯尼思的好品质：他能干、勤勉，有几分英俊。他表达爱意时那种近乎牛一般的木讷，有时也叫她恼火。她自然喜欢这样一个念头：得到一个天真男子的爱——这男子没有在别的姑娘身上跑遍情感的整个音域；然而，同样合乎情理地，她也确实指望他至少有那么一点处世的本事，能够带着每个姑娘都想要、都指望着的那一点手腕来赢得她。他追求她时那副公事公办的实在劲儿，叫她心里有气——那样子冷冷地算计着，她觉得，就像他在给哪一个病人开刀一样。在这一点上，她是冤枉了他。在肯尼思那表面平静之下，他对她的爱已烧成了一片熊熊烈火——他咒骂那层职业尊严的外壳，那外壳不知何时已反过来长进了他自己身上，成了他的一部分。

他们就这样在柔软的春夜里走着，她盼着他做那件事，而他因自己的懵懂，反而觉得那是他所能尝试的最不明智的一件事。人生便是如此，由这一桩桩自相相成的处境构成——一句话、一个眼神、一个本来微不足道的手势，便能在顷刻之间扫净堆积如山的疑虑和误解。

===Original===
Jane stood, one foot on the ground, the other on the step, her hand resting on the opened door of the car. A faintly provocative smile flitted over her face. Kenneth longed to seize this elusive, seductive girl in his arms, press her close to him, and tell her of his love. She wanted him to. Instead he steeled himself against yielding to the impulse that almost overcame him, and helped her with complete decorum into the car.…

They did not say much on the way home. Jane bade him good night, he thought somewhat coldly—as though she were vexed. He told her he was leaving the next morning for Atlanta to operate on Mrs. Tucker. She made no comment. He wondered as he drove home what he had done to offend her.…

===Chinese===
简站着，一只脚踩在地上，另一只踩在踏板上，手搁在敞开的车门上。她脸上一闪，掠过一丝若有若无、带几分撩拨的笑。肯尼思恨不得把这飘忽、诱人的姑娘一把搂进怀里，把她紧紧贴住，对她诉说他的爱。她也正盼着他这么做。可他偏偏硬下心，压下那几乎压倒他的冲动，一板一眼、规规矩矩地扶她上了车……

回家的路上他们没说几句话。简道了晚安，他觉出她有点冷淡——像是恼了的样子。他告诉她，明天一早他要去亚特兰大，给塔克太太动手术。她没接一句。他一路开车回去，一路在心里犯疑：自己到底哪儿冒犯了她……

===Original===
As he neared the house, he suddenly remembered that he had promised to look in on old Mrs. Amos, whose “rheumatics” had been giving her considerable pain. It was charity work, as she would never be able to pay him. She had sent for him several times during the day, but he had been kept so busy he had had no time to go. He was annoyed at himself for promising to call to see the quarrelsome old woman who was far more dictatorial and exacting than most of the patients who paid him promptly. With a muttered imprecation at being bothered with her just after his annoying experience with Jane and her inexplicable behaviour, he drove through the darkened streets to Mrs. Amos’ home. He found her sitting in a creaky rocking-chair. She began immediately to pour maledictions on his head for neglecting her all day. He answered her shortly, gave her her medicine, and left.

===Chinese===
快到家的时候，他忽然记起自己答应过要去看看老阿莫斯太太——她那「风湿病」最近折腾得她够呛。这是白看的义诊，她这辈子也付不起他诊金。白天她派人来请过他好几回，可他忙得脚不沾地，一直没空去。他对自己答应下来要看这个好吵架的老太婆，心里也有点不耐烦——她比绝大多数痛痛快快付诊金的病人还要颐指气使、还要挑剔得多。刚被简和她那莫名其妙的态度弄得心烦，又得去伺候这位老太太，他低低咒骂了一句，开着车穿过黑黢黢的街道，往阿莫斯太太家去。到了那儿，见她坐在一把吱嘎作响的摇椅里。她一张口便劈头盖脸地骂他，怪他一整天都不来看她。他三言两语回了几句，给她留下药，便走了。

===Original===
Carefully guiding the car through the gullies and holes in the unpaved street, he set out for home. Nearing the corner of Harris and State Streets, he heard a sound as of several automobiles. He looked down Harris Street just in time to see three closed cars stop suddenly at the corner. From one of them two white-robed figures descended, lifting a large, black bundle that seemed exceedingly heavy. This done, the figures jumped hurriedly into the car, and it with the other two speeded away in the direction from which they had come.

Kenneth, his curiosity aroused, turned his car around and drove to the spot to see what was going on. As he slowed his car at the corner, a muffled groan came from the object lying there in the street. Hastily getting down, he turned it over and in the half-light found it to be the body of a human being. His hands felt sticky. Holding them close to his face, he found them smeared with tar.

===Chinese===
他小心翼翼地把车驶过这条没铺路的街上的车辙和坑洞，动身回家。将到哈里斯街和州街的交叉口时，他听见一阵像是几辆汽车的声音。他顺着哈里斯街望过去，正看见三辆封闭的车子在街角猛地停下。其中一辆里下来两个白袍人影，抬起一大卷黑乎乎、看着极沉的东西。事一办完，那两个人影便急忙跳回车里，那辆车和另外两辆便朝来时的方向飞驰而去。

肯尼思起了疑心，把车掉过头，开到那地方去看是怎么回事。他在街角放慢车速，那躺在街上的东西发出一声闷闷的呻吟。他赶忙下车，把它翻过来，在昏暗的光线里看出这是一具人的身体。他的手感到黏糊糊的。把手凑到眼前一看，上面糊的是柏油。

===Original===
He got from his car a small flashlight. Going back to the inert mass, he turned the ray of light on the body and found it to be that of a naked woman, covered with tar yet warm to the touch. Between the dabs of the sticky mess on the woman’s back were long welts, some of them bleeding, as though a heavy-thonged whip had been applied with great force. The hair was dishevelled and in its strands were bits of the melted tar. Kenneth experienced a feeling of nausea at the revolting sight. The woman lay on her face. From her mouth and nose there ran a stream of blood which already was forming a little pool beneath her face that became bloody mud as it mixed with the dust in the road. Seizing her by her left shoulder, Kenneth half raised the body and turned his flashlight on the woman’s face. It was Nancy Ware, the wife of the Negro killed by George Parker. Half carrying, half dragging the limp form, Kenneth managed in some fashion to get Nancy to her own home a few doors away. The door stood open as though Nancy had left it for a minute to call on one of her neighbours. On the table in the front room, there stood a lamp yet burning, the chimney blackened with the soot caused by the wind blowing upon it. Beside the lamp lay a garment on which Nancy had been sewing.

===Chinese===
他从车上取来一只小手电筒。回到那不动的人形旁边，把电筒的光照在那身体上，看出这是一个赤身裸体的女人，浑身糊着柏油，摸上去却还是温的。在那女人背上，一团团黏糊的东西之间，是一道道长长的鞭痕，有的还在渗血，像是有人用了粗重的皮鞭，使劲抽打过。她的头发蓬乱，发丝间沾着一块一块融化的柏油。这副惨状令肯尼思一阵作呕。女人趴着。从她的口和鼻里淌出一道血，已在脸下的地上积成一小汪，和路面的尘土一混，成了血红的泥浆。肯尼思抓住她的左肩，把那身体半撑起来，把手电筒照向她的脸。是南希·韦尔——就是被乔治·帕克杀害的那个黑人之妻。肯尼思半抱半拖地把这软绵绵的人弄到几家门外南希自己的家里。门是开着的，像是南希出门一会儿去敲哪位邻居的门。前屋桌上还点着一盏灯，灯罩被风吹得熏黑了一层烟灰。灯旁放着一件南希正在缝补的衣裳。

===Original===
Kenneth placed her on the bed and hurried next door to summon help. His efforts were unsuccessful. He pounded on the door with both fists, calling out in his excitement to the occupants to open up. After what seemed an infinite delay, a window to the left of the door cautiously opened and an inquiring voice wanted to know what was the matter. Seeing who it was, the owner of the voice disappeared and a minute later opened the door. Kenneth hastily told what had happened, brushing aside a muttered excuse that the delay in answering was due to the fact that “I didn’ know but whut you might ’a’ been the p’lice.”

===Chinese===
肯尼思把她安顿到床上，又急忙跑到隔壁去求援。可这一趟却碰了壁。他用两只拳头擂门，激动地喊里头的人开门。等了好一段仿佛漫无尽头的工夫，门左边一扇窗子才小心翼翼地开了，一个犹疑的声音问出了什么事。看清是谁之后，那声音的主人缩了回去，过了一分钟才来开门。肯尼思匆匆讲了出了什么事，对方嘟囔了一句解释应门迟了的原因——「我哪晓得你不是警察呢」——被他一句带过。

===Original===
On going back to Nancy’s cottage, Kenneth gave her a restorative and endeavoured to relieve her suffering. She began to revive after a few minutes. In the meantime the neighbours called by Kenneth arrived, and they removed as much as they could of the tar from Nancy’s body. Kenneth then examined her back, finding it covered with long and ugly gashes that bled profusedly. He dressed them and Nancy was arranged as comfortably as possible. He found himself so tired after the hard work and excitement of the day and evening that he was almost ready to drop in his tracks. At the same time he had an uncontrollable desire to find out just what had happened to Nancy Ware. He was almost certain the Ku Klux Klan had done it, but he wanted to hear the story from Nancy’s own lips. The neighbours had gone, with the exception of an evil-looking, elderly woman who had volunteered to remain with Nancy until morning.

===Chinese===
回到南希的小屋，肯尼思给她用了强心药，又尽力减轻她的痛苦。过了几分钟，她开始缓过一点气来。其间肯尼思叫来的邻居也到了，她们便七手八脚地把南希身上能擦掉的柏油擦掉一些。肯尼思随后检查她的背，见上面布满了又长又丑、血如泉涌的伤口。他把这些伤一一处理好，又把南希尽可能安置得舒服一些。这一整天加上一晚上的辛劳和激动下来，他已经累得几乎当场就要倒下。与此同时，他又有一股按捺不住的念头，非要知道南希·韦尔到底出了什么事不可。他几乎可以断定是三K党（Ku Klux Klan）下的手，可他要从南希自己嘴里听见这事。邻居们都散了，只留下一个面相凶恶的老妇人，自告奋勇愿意陪南希到天亮。

===Original===
After the application of restoratives regularly for an hour, she began to show signs of returning consciousness. Kenneth watched her eagerly. Five minutes later her eyelids fluttered. She gave a low moan—almost a whimper. Suddenly she cried out in the terror of delirium: “Doan let ’em whip me no mo’! Doan let ’em whip me no mo?!” and writhed in her agony. She struggled to arise but Kenneth, sitting by the side of the bed, managed with the aid of the other woman to restrain Nancy and calm her. Afterwards she became more rational. Her eyes opened. In them was a gleam of recognition of Kenneth and he knew she was regaining consciousness.

Another wait. Then, at Kenneth’s questioning, she began to tell what had happened. For weeks he had thought but little of her and the tragedy that had taken place in this same house, other events having crowded it out of his mind.

===Chinese===
每小时定时地用强心药，过了一个钟头，她开始显出苏醒的迹象。肯尼思紧张地盯着她。又过五分钟，她的眼皮动了动。她低低地呻吟了一声——几乎像是一声呜咽。忽然，她在谵妄的恐惧里尖叫起来：「别让他们再打我啦！别让他们再打我啦？！」痛得在床上扭动。她挣扎着想爬起来，肯尼思坐在床边，在那位老妇人的帮助下，把南希按住、安抚下来。过后她神志清楚了一些。她睁开了眼。眼里有一道认出肯尼思的光，他知道她已苏醒过来了。

又是一阵等待。后来，在肯尼思的探问下，她开始讲起出了什么事。这几个礼拜以来，他几乎没怎么想过她，也没怎么想过发生在这同一座屋子里的那场悲剧——别的事早已把它从他的脑子里挤了出去。

===Original===
“Doc, you won’t let ’em get me again, will you?” she pleaded with a whimper like a child’s. Kenneth assured her he wouldn’t.

“Doc, I ain’t done nuthin’ t’ them Kluxers. Hones’ t’ Gawd, I ain’t.” Kenneth told her soothingly that he knew she hadn’t.

===Chinese===
「大夫，你不会让他们再把我抓走吧，会不会？」她像孩子似的呜咽着央求。肯尼思向她保证，不会的。

「大夫，我啥也没对他们三K党徒做过呀。对天发誓，真的没有。」肯尼思柔声安慰她，说他知道她没做过。

===Original===
“I was jes’ sittin’ here tendin’ to my own business when dey come a rap on de do’. W’en I open de do’, dere wuz two o; dem Kluxers standin’ dere—befo’ I could holler dey grab me and put a rag in my mouf.” A shudder passed through her body as the terror came back to her at the memory of what she had been through.

“Dey put me in a automobile and ca’ied me way out yonder in de woods by de fact’ry. Dey pull all my clo’es off me and den dey whip me till I couldn’ stan’ up no mo’. Den dey tell me I been talkin’ too much. Doc, I ain’t said a word t’ nobdy ’cept dat dey oughter do somethin’t that man George Parker for killin’ my man Bud.… Den dey po’ed tar all over me and kick me and spit on me some mo’.… Said I oughter had mo’ sense dan t’ talk ’bout no white gemmen. Oh—oh—oh—ain’t dey nothin’ to he’p us po’ cullud fo’ks—ain’t dey nobody—ain’t dey nobody?”

===Chinese===
「我就坐在这儿，管着我自个儿的事，他们就来敲门。我一开门，外头站着两个三K党徒——我还没来得及喊，他们就上来揪住我，拿块破布塞住了我的嘴。」一阵战栗穿过她的身体——那一夜经历的恐怖，又随回忆一齐涌了回来。

「他们把我塞进一辆汽车，拉到工厂那边老远的林子里。他们把我衣裳全扒光了，就抽我，抽到我站都站不起来。然后他们说我话太多。大夫，我啥也没跟谁说过呀，我就只说了一句：杀了我家巴德的那个乔治·帕克，他们该拿他怎么办才是。……他们就往我身上浇柏油，又踢我，又朝我吐唾沫。……说我不该长那点脑子，去说一个白人老爷的闲话。哦——哦——哦——就没人能帮帮我们这些苦命的有色人吗——就没人——就没人吗？」

===Original===
It was just as Kenneth had suspected. Good God, and these were the self-elected defenders of morals in the South! What if Nancy wasn’t all that she should have been?—whose was the greater fault—hers or George Parker’s? He could see him now in the bank—smug, a hypocritical smile on his face, talking about what the white people have got to do to stop these troublesome “niggers” from getting too cheeky—about protecting “pure” Southern womanhood from attacks by “black, burly brutes.” And the Klan with all its boasted and advertised chivalry—twenty or thirty strong men to beat up and maltreat one lone woman, because she “talked too much” about the brutal, cold-blooded murder of her husband! Kenneth’s optimism over the organization of the cooperative societies began to cool—in its stead there came a blind, unreasoning hatred and furious rage against the men who had done this deed to Nancy Ware. God, but he would have given anything he owned to get them all together and kill them one by one—slowly, with all the tortures he could devise! The damned, cowardly devils! The filthy, smug-faced hypocrites!

Nancy was resting easily Kenneth, shaken by the fury of his anger, more devastating because he knew that he could do nothing but hurl silent imprecations on the heads of those who had done this deed—impotent because his skin was black and he lived in the South—went home to roll and toss during the few hours of the night which remained before he took the train to Atlanta.

===Chinese===
果不其然，正如肯尼思所料。老天爷，这一帮人，就是南方自封的道德捍卫者！就算南希不是什么清白女人——那更大的罪过该算在谁头上——是她，还是乔治·帕克？他如今还能看见他在那银行里——一脸得意，挂着一副虚伪的笑，谈着白人该怎么办才能挡住这些麻烦的「黑鬼（nigger）」[4]，叫他们别再不知天高地厚——谈着要保护「纯洁」的南方女人，免遭「黑皮肤、壮硕的野兽」的侵犯。而那三K党，吹得天花乱坠、四处张扬的骑士精神——竟要二三十个壮汉去围殴、凌辱一个孤身女人，因为她竟「话太多」，谈了她丈夫被残暴、冷血地杀害一事！肯尼思对于组织合作社的那份乐观，开始凉下去——取而代之的，是一种盲目的、无理性的仇恨，是对这些向南希·韦尔下此毒手之人的一腔狂怒。老天爷，他恨不得豁出自己所有的一切，把他们统统抓到一处，一个一个慢慢地弄死——用上他所想得出的所有酷刑！这帮该死的、怯懦的恶鬼！这帮肮脏的、一脸得意的伪君子！

南希安安稳稳地睡着了。肯尼思却被自己怒火的烈度震撼——而这烈度更叫他崩溃，因为他明白，自己除了在心里默念咒骂这些下毒手的人，什么也做不了——这般无力，是因为他的皮肤是黑的，又住在南方——他回了家，在搭火车去亚特兰大之前那短短几个钟头的夜里，翻来覆去，再也睡不着。

---

> 译注：
>
> [1] **但以理（A Daniel）**：典出《旧约·但以理书》。但以理是犹太被掳至巴比伦的少年，因智慧与解梦之能居异邦高位，被视为领袖与审判者。此处喻肯尼思如一位先知般在他们当中兴起、引领族人。「A Daniel come to judgement」亦为莎剧《威尼斯商人》第四幕中之名句，已成英语喻指「智慧卓越之裁断者/领袖」的典故。
>
> [2] **马其顿人的呼声（Macedonian cry）**：典出《新约·使徒行传》16:9，保罗夜间见异象，「有一个马其顿人站着求他说：『请你过到马其顿来帮助我们。』」后成英语典故，喻来自远方之迫切求助呼声。此处指肯尼思听见族人的呼救并作出回应。
>
> [3] **反舌鸟（mockingbird）**：北美常见的模仿鸟，善仿他鸟啼鸣及环境声响，歌声婉转多变。南方春夜常见，其啼鸣在本书中是南方田园夜色的标志意象。
>
> [4] **nigger（黑鬼）**：此章多处涉及种族贬语与三K党暴行。①银行出纳乔治·帕克口中的「niggers」与「black, burly brutes」，系白人种族主义对黑人的蔑称与污名化刻板印象，作者怀特借此揭露三K党所谓「保护南方女性纯洁」之说的虚伪——乔治·帕克本人即与南希通奸、并枪杀其夫巴德（第6章），却以「黑鬼勃然兴事」「野兽侵犯白人女子」等陈词滥调为自身及白人暴行开脱；②三K党徒以「白人老爷（white gemmen）」自称、对南希施以柏油鞭刑，其「保护白人女性」之意识形态与实际暴行之荒谬反差，作者以冷峻反讽笔调直陈。译文一律保留「黑鬼（nigger）」「有色人（coloured）」之原貌与贬义冲击力不软化，与第1章以来全书处理一致（同杜波依斯项目原则）。又，南希口述遭遇一段用黑人乡村方言（de/do’/dem/mouf/ca’ied/po’ed/he’p/cullud 等），译文以中文乡土口吻传达其惊惧与质朴，不用怪异拼写（与第6章巴德临终口吻、第8章塔克/特雷西陈述、第12章塔克建议之口吻一致）。

## XIV / 十四

===Original===
It seemed to Kenneth he had just fallen into a troubled slumber when he was aroused by the tinkling of the telephone bell at the side of his bed. It was Hiram Tucker.

“Doc, I reck’n you won’t have to go t’ Atlanty today, after all. My wife, she tol’ me to tell you she’s changed her min’ ’bout that op’ration.… What’s dat?… Naw, suh, she’s kinder skeered she won’ wake up from dat chlo’form.… Yas, suh, yas, suh, I knows ’rangements been made but, Doc, you ain’t married, so you don’t know nuthin’ ’tall ’bout wimmenfolks.… Some day you’ll learn dat when dey says dey ain’t gwine do somethin’ dey’s done sot dere minds on not doing, dey ain’t gwine t’ do it.… Hello.… Hello.… Hello!”

But Kenneth had hung up. He telephoned the local telegraph office to send a wire to the hospital in Atlanta to cancel the arrangements he had made for the operation on the following day, and tumbled back in bed to sleep like a log until late in the morning. He was awakened by Bob, who informed him that the reception room was half filled with patients who were no longer patient at being kept waiting so long. He arose reluctantly, his eyes still filled with sleep. Bob leaned against the wall, hands in pockets, and looked at his brother with a smile of amusement. Kenneth, not thoroughly awakened as yet, paid no attention to him for a time, but at last noticed Bob’s smile.

===Chinese===
肯尼思觉得自己才刚刚坠入一段烦躁的浅睡，就被床边电话铃声叮铃铃地吵醒了。是海勒姆·塔克。

「大夫，我寻思您今儿到底还是不用上亚特兰大去了。我那口子，她叫我跟您说，那手术她改主意不做了。……您说啥？......不，先生，她就是有点儿怕，怕吸了那氯仿（chlo’form）[1]醒不过来。……是，先生，是，先生，我知道都安排好了，可大夫，您没成过家，您一点儿也不懂女人家的事。……赶明儿您就明白了，她们要是说自个儿不做一件事，那就是铁了心不做了，再也不会去做的......喂......喂......喂！」

可肯尼思已经把电话挂了。他给本地电报局打电话，让他们给亚特兰大的医院拍份电报，取消他为次日手术所做的全部安排，然后一头栽回床上，睡得像个死人，一直睡到日头老高。是鲍勃把他叫醒的，告诉他候诊室里已经坐了半屋子病人，等了这么久，都等得不耐烦了。他不情愿地爬起来，眼睛里还满是睡意。鲍勃靠在墙上，两手插在口袋里，带着一脸看好戏的笑望着哥哥。肯尼思还没彻底醒透，有一阵子没理会他，可到底还是注意到了鲍勃的笑。

===Original===
“Why this early morning humour? I’ve seen many possums with a more engaging smile then the one that distorts your face now!” he half-grumblingly, half-cheerfully observed.

Bob but grinned the more at Kenneth’s remark.

“I was just thinking that if Jane could only get one glimpse of you in the morning before breakfast, your chances would be mighty slim with her.”

“Jane? What have my looks to do with her?” Kenneth retorted with some heat, in a vain attempt to spar for time.

Bob addressed the world in general, calling on it for some aid in understanding this brother of his.

“Jane?” he mimicked Kenneth’s tone of surprise. “You talk like a ten-year-old boy with his first love affair. Isn’t he the innocent one, though? Why, you poor maligned creature, everybody in Central City who isn’t blind knows that you are head over heels in love with Jane Phillips. And,” he added as an afterthought, “those who are blind have been told it. But to return to my original observation, if there was some means by which, with all propriety, all the girls in the world who are in love could see, and be seen by, the poor boobs with whom they are so infatuated, marriage-licence bureaus would be closed that day, never to open again.” This last with an omniscient air of worldly wisdom that caused Kenneth to burst into a roar of laughter, while Bob watched him, somewhat discomfited.

===Chinese===
「这一大清早的，你乐什么？我见过好多负鼠，笑起来都比你这会儿扭歪的脸讨人喜欢得多！」他半是嘟囔、半是快活地说。

可鲍勃听了肯尼思这话，反而笑得更欢了。

「我刚才在想，要是简能瞅见你早饭前这副早起的模样，你追她的胜算可就小得可怜喽。」

「简？我的模样跟她有什么相干？」肯尼思带点火气地反唇相讥，徒劳地想拖一拖时间。

鲍勃冲着整个天地发话，求它帮忙参一参他这位哥哥。

「简？」他学肯尼思那惊讶的腔调，「你说话就像个十岁的小男孩，碰上头一桩恋爱的事。他可真够天真的啊！哎，你这受了天大冤枉的可怜虫，中央城里只要不是瞎子，谁不知道你死心塌地地爱上了简·菲利普斯。还有呢，」他想了想又补一句，「瞎的也已经有人告诉他们了。可话又说回我方才那句话，要是有个法子，又能顾全体面，又能让全世界所有正谈着恋爱的姑娘，瞧见、也被瞧见她们爱得死去活来的那些个傻小子，那结婚登记处那天就得关门，再也别想开张了。」最后这几句，他带着一种无所不知的世故老成，惹得肯尼思哄然大笑，鲍勃望着他，多少有点下不来台。

===Original===
“What’re you laughing at?” he demanded in an aggrieved tone. Kenneth laughed all the harder. “Why, you poor little innocent, you haven’t gotten rid of your pin feathers, and yet you are talking as though you were a philosopher like Schopenhauer. You’d better wait until you finish school and see something of the world. Then you can talk a little—though only a little as you did just now. By the way, it’s about time for you to be planning for school this fall. Still thinking about going back to Atlanta?”

“I don’t know what I want to do,” was Bob’s troubled rejoinder. “I’ve seen too much of what’s going on around this town since papa died to be satisfied with school again. I’ve probably seen more of the real sordidness and meanness and deviltry of this place since I’ve been settling up papa’s affairs than you’ll see in five years. At any rate, I hope you don’t,” he finished somewhat doubtfully.

“Bob”—Kenneth walked over and put his arm around his brother’s shoulders—“the trouble with you is that you’re too darned sensitive. I know things aren’t all they ought to be around here, but we’ve got to buckle down and make them that way. And perhaps I’ve seen more of this deviltry than you think.”

He told Bob of what had happened to Nancy Ware the night before. A long whistle of surprise escaped from Bob’s lips.

===Chinese===
「你笑什么？」他愤愤地问。肯尼思反而笑得更厉害了。「哎呀，你这可怜的天真小子，你那身雏毛还没换干净呢，就说话活像个叔本华那样的哲学家了。你最好还是等念完书、见过点世面再说。到那时候你才能讲上一点——也不过像你刚才那样一点点罢了。顺便说一句，你也该筹划今年秋天开学的事了。还是想回亚特兰大去吗？」

「我不知道自己想干什么，」鲍勃忧心忡忡地答道，「自从爸爸过世，我在这镇上见到的那些事太多了，再回学校念书我安不下心。我料理爸爸后事这些日子，见到的这地方真正的肮脏、卑劣、恶毒，多半比你五年里见到的还多。无论如何，我希望你别见到，」他末了又犹疑地补了一句。

「鲍勃，」——肯尼思走过去，一只手搭在弟弟肩上——「你的毛病就是太过敏了。我知道这地方的事不像该有的样子，可咱们得沉下心来，把它扭成那个样子。再说了，这种恶毒的事，我见过的也许比你以为的多。」

他把头天夜里南希·韦尔的遭遇讲给鲍勃听。鲍勃的唇间溜出一声长长的、惊讶的口哨。

===Original===
“And this happened right here in the coloured section?” he asked in surprise. Kenneth nodded in assent.

“I felt they were planning some mischief but I didn’t think they would have the nerve to come right here in ‘Darktown’ and do it. I wonder,” he said musingly, “if that dirty little Jim Archer who said those filthy things to Minnie Baxter that day is a member of the Klan. I passed him on Lee Street this morning and he grinned at me like a cat that has just eaten a fat mouse.”

“He may be,” Kenneth replied. “Nancy Ware told me last night she recognized the voices of Sheriff Parker and Henry Lane and George Parker and two or three other prominent white people here.”

“That settles it,” Bob answered determinedly. “When you first came back here I thought you were foolish to do so after having been in France. I said I was going to get out of this country as soon as I could and live in France or Brazil or any old place where a man isn’t judged by the colour of his skin. But I’ve decided that I’d be a coward if I did run away like that. Ken,” he said in voice that showed he had passed in spite of his years from childhood into the more serious things of manhood, “I’m going to Harvard this fall. I’m going to take whatever course I need to get into the law school. I’m going to make myself the best lawyer they can turn out. And then I’m coming back here to the South like you did and give my time to fighting for my people!”

Bob’s eyes flashed. In them was a light of high resolve such a look as might have shone in the eyes of Garibaldi or of Joan of Arc.

Kenneth said nothing, but he gripped Bob’s hand in his and there passed between the two brothers a look of mutual understanding and sympathy that was more potent and meaningful than words.

===Chinese===
「这事就发生在有色人区？」他惊讶地问。肯尼思点头称是。

「我原觉着他们在打什么坏主意，可没想到他们竟敢直接跑到『黑镇』[2]来动手。我纳闷，」他若有所思地说，「那天对明妮·巴克斯特说那些下流话的那个肮脏的小吉姆·阿切尔，是不是三K党里的人。今早我在李街跟他打了个照面，他冲我咧嘴一笑，活像一只刚吞下肥老鼠的猫。」

「可能是，」肯尼思答道，「南希·韦尔昨夜告诉我，她听出了帕克警长、亨利·莱恩、乔治·帕克，还有本地两三个显赫白人的嗓子。」

「这就定了，」鲍勃毅然答道，「你刚回来那阵子，我想你在法国待过还回来，真是犯傻。我说过我要尽早离开这地方，去法国、巴西，随便哪个不拿肤色论人的旮旯都成。可如今我拿定了主意，要就这么跑了，我便是懦夫。肯，」他以一种嗓音说道——这嗓音显出他虽年纪还轻，却已从童年跨入了成年人那些更正经的事——「今年秋天我要上哈佛（Harvard）去。我要把进法学院该念的课程都念了。我要把自己造就成他们能培养出来的最好的律师。然后我要像你一样回到南方来，把我的时间献给我的人民！」

鲍勃眼睛发亮。眼里那道崇高决心的光，一如从前或许曾在加里波第、在圣女贞德眼中闪耀过的那样。

肯尼思什么也没说，只是把鲍勃的手握在自己手里。两兄弟之间交换了一个彼此心照、彼此怜惜的眼神，那比任何言语都更有力、更深长。

===Original===
Kenneth went down to attend to his patients and nothing more was said of the incident between them. Bob took on a new interest in life. His moodiness, his brooding over the constant irritations and insults he had to suffer in his dealings as a coloured man with the whites of the town, his resentment at the attitude of condescension on the part of the poor and ignorant whites who had neither his intelligence, his education, nor his wealth—all these disappeared in his eager preparations for the new life he had mapped out for himself. He already saw himself a powerful champion of his race and he gloried in that vision with all of the impetuosity and idealistic fervour of youth.

As for Kenneth, he divided his time between his practice, Jane, and the formation of more branches of the N.N.F.C.P.L.

Kenneth knew there was nothing to be done towards the punishment of the men who had so brutally beaten Nancy Ware. He knew that it would even be unwise for him to talk too much about it. If Sheriff Parker was himself a member of the Klan, reporting the outrage to him would be in effect a serving of notice that he was meddling in the affairs of the Klan which might bring disastrous results at a time when Kenneth was most anxious to avoid such a complication, certainly until the cooperative societies were well under way and actively functioning. Much as he chafed under the restraint and at his own impotence in the situation, Kenneth knew that his interference would be a useless and foolhardy butting of his head against a stone wall.

===Chinese===
肯尼思下楼去照看他的病人，他俩之间再没提起这件事。鲍勃对生活有了一番新的兴致。他那阴郁，他因身为有色人、在本镇与白人打交道时不得不忍受的层出不穷的气恼和侮辱而生的郁结，他对那些既无他的才智、又无他的教养、也无他的家产、却偏偏摆出一副居高临下架子的穷而无知的白人的愤懑——这一切，都消融在他为自己规划的新生活所作的热切准备之中了。他已经看见自己成了本族一位强有力的捍卫者，他怀着少年人全部的冲劲和理想主义的热忱，为这一幅前景欢欣鼓舞。

至于肯尼思，他把时间分作三处：行医、简，以及把 N.N.F.C.P.L.[3]的更多分部办起来。

肯尼思知道，要惩罚那些如此凶残地毒打南希·韦尔的人，是无计可施的。他甚至知道，这事自己说太多都不明智。倘若帕克警长本人就是三K党徒，那么把这场暴行去报告他，实际上等于通知对方自己正在插手三K党的事，这在肯尼思最怕节外生枝的当口——至少在各合作社尚未站稳脚跟、切实运转起来之前——可能招来灾难性的后果。尽管这重束缚和他自己在此事上的无能为力令他焦灼难耐，肯尼思心里清楚，他的干预不过是拿脑袋去撞石墙，既无用又冒失。

===Original===
It occurred to him to tell what had happened to Judge. Stevenson. He could be trusted and was as much opposed to the outlawry of the Klan as Kenneth himself. The judge listened gravely to the end without comment other than a question here and there. “That looks worse than I thought,” he said half to himself. “A few mo’ cracks like that and there’ll be hell to pay ’round here. But ’twon’t do no good for you t’ meddle in it,” he observed in answer to Kenneth’s question as to what he could do. “If Nancy’s right about Bob Parker being in it, your sayin’ anything will only set them on you. You’d better go ahead and get your societies on their feet and then you’ll have somethin’ behin’ you. Then you won’t be playin’ a lone hand.”

As for the coloured people, there were several days of excited gossip over what had happened to Nancy Ware. There was not much to go on, as she had been so frightened by her terrible experience that she refused for once to talk. The only tangible effect was that mysterious parcels marked with the names of household implements began to arrive at the homes of the coloured people, but which contained firearms and ammunition. There was also a noticeable tightening of the lips and the development of a less cordial relationship between white and black. Negroes, feeling that there was no help they could expect from the law, felt that their backs were being slowly pressed against the wall. Within a few hours the old *esprit cordial* between white and black had been wiped out. Negroes who had been happy-go-lucky, carefree, and kindly in manner began to talk among themselves of “dying fighting” if forced to the limit.

===Chinese===
他想到把事情原原本本告诉史蒂文森法官。法官靠得住，对三K党的无法无天，他和肯尼思一样深恶痛绝。法官神情凝重地听到末了，只在这里那里插问一句，没作别的评论。「这看起来比我预想的还糟，」他半是自言自语地说，「再这么来几下，这一带可就要大难临头了。不过你去插一手，没半点好处，」他是这样回答肯尼思该怎么办的问话的，「要是南希没认错人，鲍勃·帕克真在里头，那你一说出去，反倒把他们引到你身上来了。你不如先把你的那些团体办起来立住脚，到那时你身后就有了依仗。到那时你也就不是一个人单打独斗了。」

至于有色人这边，南希·韦尔的事闹得他们兴奋地议论了好几天。可也议论不出什么来，她被那场可怖的遭遇吓坏了，破天荒头一回不肯开口。唯一看得见摸得着的影响是：一个个贴着家用器皿名目的神秘包裹，开始送进有色人各家各户的门，里面装的都是枪支弹药。嘴唇也明显地抿紧了，黑白之间原本的关系也不再那么热络。黑人们（Negroes）觉得求诸法律无望，觉得自己正被人一寸一寸地把背脊往墙上摁。几个钟头之内，黑白之间旧日那种*由衷的亲睦*（*esprit cordial*）[4]便荡然无存。那些一向随遇而安、无忧无虑、待人厚道和气的黑人，开始彼此议论起：被逼到尽头，便「拼死一战」。

===Original===
July came with all its heat. August passed with yet more heat. With the coming of September there had been formed in Smith County alone seven branch societies of the Cooperative and Protective League with a membership of more than twelve hundred. Kenneth worked as one inspired, one who knew neither heat nor cold, fatigue nor hunger. During the day he was busy with his practice, but it mattered not how busy he had been, he was always ready and willing to drive five, ten, fifteen miles at night to aid in establishing new branches or directing and guiding and advising those already established.

The Ashland Branch, through the hard work of Hiram Tucker and Tom Tracy, had enrolled three hundred and fifteen members. In its treasury it had $657.85, to which it was constantly adding as new members were enrolled. At a meeting held during the latter part of August the members decided that they would forgo the purchasing of their supplies in bulk that year but would use the money raised towards prosecuting one of the cases of dishonest settlements when the time came for such settlements, usually in December or January. This step was decided upon after due and lengthy deliberation, as it was felt that if they could end the cheating of the farmers through court action, then these same farmers would have more money through the settlement of their accounts for the present season and could then begin the cooperative buying and distribution the following year.

===Chinese===
七月带着它全部的酷暑来了。八月过去了，暑气更甚。到了九月，光史密斯县一地，就已经成立了合作保护同盟的七个分部，会员总数超过一千二百人。肯尼思干得像个受了感召的人，不知寒暑，不知疲倦，不知饥饿。白天他忙着看诊，可无论白天有多忙，夜里他总是欣然驱车五里、十里、十五里，去帮着建立新分部，或是去指导、指点、参谋那些已经建起来的分部。

阿什兰分部，靠海勒姆·塔克和汤姆·特雷西的苦干，已经招募了三百一十五名会员。库房里有 657.85 元的公积金，还随着新会员的加入在不断添增。八月底开的一次会上，会员们议定：这一年他们暂不集中批量采购物资，而把筹集起来的钱用在来日清算时打一桩东家欺账的官司——这种清算通常在十二月或一月。这一步是经过郑重而长久的商议才定下的，因为他们觉得，要是能借打官司了结对农户的欺蒙，那么这些农户在今季清算账目时就能多拿到些钱，第二年也就可以着手合作采买和分发了。

===Original===
News of the new society that was going to end the unsatisfactory relations sharecroppers had with their landlords spread rapidly throughout the surrounding counties. Letters, crudely and cumbersomely worded and with atrocious spelling, came to Kenneth and often individuals came in person to ask that he come to their counties to organize societies there. Kenneth was elated at this sign of interest. He had expected a great deal of opposition from the coloured farmers. Bickering and carping criticism there was aplenty, but most of them regarded him as a new Moses to lead them into the promised land of economic independence. Minor disputes over authority in the local societies there were in abundance. But none of them was hard to settle, for the members themselves were too eager to get out of bondage to tolerate much petty politics and selfishness on the part of their officers.

As a loyal ally Kenneth learned to rely on Jane more and more. Often she went with him to attend meetings and to talk to groups not yet organized. While Kenneth talked to the men, Jane circulated among the women, who were subtly flattered that one so daintily clad and well educated should spend so much of her time and energy talking to lowly ones like themselves.

Her mother’s health had not been of the best during the summer. That had been throughout the summer her only worry. In August her mother had suffered an attack of paralysis, her second one. Jane decided to remain at home instead of going to Oberlin to resume her music. Dr. Bennett had been dismissed and Kenneth was now treating Mrs. Phillips. During her more serious illness in August, Jane often sat on one side of her mother’s bed until late in the night while Kenneth sat on the other, ministering to the aged woman’s wants. There came a new and stronger feeling of companionship between the two. Often Kenneth would look up suddenly and catch in Jane’s eyes a new tenderness. Without knowing what it meant, he felt a subtly conveyed encouragement in them.

===Chinese===
这个即将了结佃农与东家之间那不尽如人意关系的新团体，其名声迅速传遍了周围各县。一封封措辞粗笨累赘、拼得一塌糊涂的信寄到肯尼思手里，还常有人亲自上门，求他去他们县里也把团体办起来。肯尼思为这种热切的迹象欢欣鼓舞。他原本料想有色人农户那边会有大量的反对意见。口角和吹毛求疵的挑剔倒也着实不少，可他们大多把他看作一位新的摩西（Moses）[5]，要引领他们走进经济上独立的应许之地。各地分部内部争权夺利的小纠纷也屡见不鲜。但没有一桩是难办的，因为会员们自己急于挣脱桎梏，也就容不下他们的干事搞多少 petty 的权术和私心。

作为一位忠实的盟友，肯尼思学会了越来越多地倚重简。她常随他一起出席集会，向尚未组织起来的人群讲话。肯尼思同男人们谈，简便在妇女中间周旋——这么一位衣裳讲究、教养又好的姑娘，竟肯花那么多时间和气力，同她们这样卑微的人说话，她们心里隐隐觉着受宠若惊。

整个夏天，她母亲身子骨一直不大好。那是她整个夏天唯一的烦心事。八月里她母亲又发了一次瘫痪（paralysis），这是第二次了。简决定留在家里，不去奥伯林（Oberlin）续修她的音乐了。贝内特医生已被辞退，如今由肯尼思来给菲利普斯太太看病。八月她病得更重的那阵子，简常常坐在母亲床的一边直到深夜，肯尼思坐在另一边，照料这位老妇人的种种需要。两人之间生出了一种新的、更深切的伙伴之情。肯尼思常常蓦地一抬头，撞见简眼里一种新的温柔。他不知道那是什么意思，却从中觉出一种隐隐传来的鼓励。

===Original===
He had, however, spoken no word of love to her, preferring to bide his time until a propitious occasion arose. He had told her that he loved her—had he not done so, she would have known—he was content to wait until she could decide what she wanted to do. At times the task was hard not to tell her again and again of his love. Often as she sat by his side and talked of inconsequential things, he would again be seized by that consuming impulse to sweep away all her objections and demolish by the very violence of his love the obstacles that held him back from possessing her. He found himself more and more filled with a wonderment that bordered on dismay as he tried to suppress this devastating longing with less success every time this feeling came over him. He tried staying away from Jane. At first he had seen her but once a week and that on Sunday evenings. Then he began dropping by to see her on Wednesdays. Of late his visits had numbered three and four a week. On those nights when he was away, he was restless and irritable. This became so noticeable that Mamie threatened jokingly one night to go over and beg Jane to marry Kenneth or throw him down hard or anything that would make him less like a bear around the house. She and Jane had become fast friends—which pleased Kenneth not a little, as it meant that Jane would be more frequently in the house than otherwise would have been the case.

As for Jane, in spite of herself, she found herself more and more interested in Kenneth and the things he was doing. She found herself eagerly looking forward to the evenings when he called. She wondered if she were entirely honest in seeing so much of him.

Why didn’t Kenneth say something now? She felt rather annoyed at him for being so considerate. With a woman’s prerogative of inconsistency, she resented his obeying so implicitly her demand that he wait until she had made up her mind. Men were so silly—you told them to do a thing and they went like fools and did it. Why didn’t he talk about something else besides his old cooperative societies and the Ku Klux Klan and his old hospital and what that old Judge Stevenson had said to him that day? Life is such a funny thing.

But Kenneth went along his way, not even suspecting what was going on in Jane’s mind. He was like the majority of men—wise in their own minds but amazingly naive and ignorant when they left the beaten paths of everyday affairs.

===Chinese===
然而他始终没对她说过一句情话，宁可静待良机。他已经告诉过她他爱她——他若不说，她也早知道了——他甘心等到她拿定主意自己想怎么办。可有时，忍住不一遍又一遍对她倾诉爱意，是件难事。她常坐在他身旁，谈着些无关紧要的小事，他便又会被那噬人的冲动攫住，想一把扫开她所有的推拒，想凭他爱情本身的猛烈，去摧毁那阻挡他得到她的一切障碍。他发现自己越来越被一种近乎沮丧的惊奇所充满——因为每当这种感觉袭来，他试图压下这销魂的渴念，却一次比一次更不成功。他试着躲着简。起初他一周只见她一次，还是在礼拜天晚上。后来他开始在礼拜三也顺道去看看她。近来他一周要登门三四次了。那些见不着她的夜晚，他坐立不安，烦躁易怒。这事明显得连玛米有一晚都打趣说，要亲自过去求简嫁给肯尼思，要么就狠狠甩了他，要么想个什么法子都成，好让他别再整天像头熊似的在家里转。她和简已经成了要好的朋友——这让肯尼思颇为高兴，因为这意味着简会比从前更常到家里来。

至于简，她不由自主地发现自己越来越对肯尼思、对他做的那些事感兴趣。她发现自己热切地盼着他来访的那些晚上。她怀疑自己这样频频见他，是不是太不诚实了。

肯尼思怎么这会儿还不说点什么？她倒有点生他的气，嫌他太体贴了。带着女人那份前后不一的特权，她竟怨他如此听话地遵从了她「等她拿定主意」的要求。男人真傻——你叫他们做一件事，他们就像傻子一样跑去做。他怎么就不会说点别的，别老讲他那些合作社啊、三K党啊、他那所医院啊、那天老史蒂文森法官跟他说了些什么啊？生活真是件古怪的事。

可肯尼思照旧走他自己的路，压根儿没疑心简心里在想什么。他和大多数男人一样——在自己熟悉的领域里自以为挺明白，一旦离开日常事务那些走熟了的老路，便天真无知得惊人。

===Original===
The end of the first week in September came. Bob had completed all arrangements to leave the following week for Cambridge, there to take his entrance examinations, after studying for them all summer. Kenneth had written to an old friend there who had made the necessary negotiations. Bob was an entirely new individual from the one he had been when Kenneth had returned to Central City. His air of moody resentment had been replaced by an eager earnestness to begin the course he had planned for himself. The bond had grown closer between him and Kenneth, and many hours they spent together discussing and planning for the years to come. Often the two brothers and Mamie, sometimes Mrs. Harper also, sat until far in the night talking of the future. If Mamie felt saddened by the broader and more active life her brothers were planning which she, as a woman, was denied, it never showed on her face or in her voice. She might have been married long before in fact, there had been three or four men who wanted to marry her. None of them would she have. Decent enough men they were. But she was unwilling to settle down to the humdrum life of marriage with a man so far beneath her in intelligence, in ideals, in education. Being a normal, warmhearted human being, naturally she often pictured to herself what marriage in Central City would be like. But, keenly sensitive and ambitious, she shrank from marrying the type of men available, farmers, small merchants, and the like—she shuddered when she visualized herself bearing children to such a man to be brought up in a place like Central City. She yearned for love and as steadfastly put it from her. There are thousands of tragedies—for tragedy it is—like Mamie’s in the South, and the world knows it not. When Kenneth or Bob teased her about marrying, she answered him with a brave and all-concealing smile-all-concealing, that is, to masculine eyes. Only her mother and Jane knew her secret, and their lips were sealed in the bond which women seldom, if ever, break.…

===Chinese===
九月的头一个礼拜过去了。鲍勃已经办妥了一应事宜，下周就要动身去坎布里奇，在那儿参加入学考试——他为此温习了一整个夏天。肯尼思给那儿的一位老朋友写了信，由那位朋友出面做了必要的接洽。鲍勃已经和肯尼思刚回中央城那时判若两人。他那阴郁愤懑的神气，已经被一股急于开始自己既定课程的诚挚热切所取代。他和肯尼思之间的情谊也更亲近了，两人一起度过了许多时辰，讨论和筹划着往后的岁月。兄弟俩和玛米三个，有时还有哈珀太太，常常坐到深夜，谈着将来。如果玛米为兄弟俩筹划的那更广阔、更主动的生活而暗自伤感——那样的生活，她身为一个女人是无份的——那也从未流露在她的脸上或声音里。她其实早该嫁人了，事实上，有过三四个男人想娶她。她一个都不要。他们也都是体面人。可她不甘心嫁给一个在才智、在理想、在教养上远远不如自己的人，去过那种平淡如水的婚姻生活。作为一个正常的、热心肠的人，她自然常常在心中描摹在中央城结婚成家会是什么样子。然而，她又敏感又心高，一想到要嫁给身边这些拿得出手的男人——农夫啦、小商人啦之类——便退缩了；她一设想自己要给这样一个男人生儿育女、把他们拉扯在中央城这样的地方长大，便不寒而栗。她渴望爱情，又同样坚决地把这渴望推开。南方有成千上万这样的悲剧——它确是悲剧——像玛米这样的，而世人并不知道。每逢肯尼思或鲍勃拿出嫁的事打趣她，她便以一个勇敢的、把一切遮掩得严严实实的笑容作答——所谓把一切遮掩得严严实实，是对男人的眼睛而言。只有她母亲和简知道她的秘密，而她们的嘴在女人之间那条少有违背的盟约里封着……

===Original===
That night Jane looked better than Kenneth had ever seen her look before. They seldom went out except for a short ride in his car. For there was no place to which they could go. Central City boasted one place of public amusement—the Idle Hour Moving Picture Palace. And to that no Negro could go. Once they had admitted Negroes to the gallery. None of the better element ever went, as they had to go through a dark and foul-smelling alleyway to reach the entrance they had to use. The type of Negroes whose pride permitted them to go were so boisterous and laughed so loud that even they were soon barred.

As usual they sat on the vine-covered porch where a breath of cool air was more likely to be had than in the parlour. That day he had had one of his more frequently recurring spells when he felt that he could not keep his promise a day longer to wait until Jane had made up her mind. At first he had thought of telephoning her and saying that he was ill or busy—any old excuse to stay away. But he wanted to see her too much for that patent evasion. He would go over to see her but would talk of nothing but business or cooperative societies. That’s it, he would keep in “safe” territory. But Jane had never looked more lovely than on that particular night. Kenneth’s heart jumped as he greeted her after she had kept him waiting just the right length of time. He likened her instinctively to a flame-coloured flower of rare beauty. All of the suppressed passion surged upward in him. He felt himself slipping. He turned away to gain control of himself. Had he not done so, he would have seen the swift look of disappointment on her face at his restraint.

===Chinese===
那天晚上，简比肯尼思从未见过的任何时候都要好看。除了坐他的车出去兜一小会儿风，他们难得出门。因为他们也无处可去。中央城只有一处可以夸耀的公共娱乐场所——「闲暇时光」影剧院。可那地方，黑人去不得。从前他们也准许黑人上楼座。可体面些的人谁也不去，因为要走到那个专给他们走的入口，非得穿过一条又黑又臭的窄巷不可。而那些自尊心容得下他们去的那一类黑人，又吵又闹，笑得又响，弄得连他们也很快被拒之门外了。

他们照旧坐在爬满藤蔓的门廊上，那里比客厅里更易得一丝凉风。那天，他又犯了那阵子里越来越频繁发作的老毛病——他觉得自己再也不能多忍一天，再也守不住「等简拿定主意」这个诺言了。起初他想打电话给她，说自己病了或忙——随便找个什么老借口躲着不去。可他太想见她了，那样的明摆着的推托根本行不通。他要过去看她，但只谈正事，只谈合作社。就这样，他要把自己圈在「安全」地带。可偏偏那天夜里，简比哪一回都妩媚。她让他恰到好处地等了那么一会儿，随后他向她问好时，心猛地一跳。他本能地把她比作一朵火焰色、美得罕见的奇花。所有被压抑下去的激情一齐往上涌。他觉得自己在往下溜。他转过脸去，好镇定一下自己。他要是不转开，就会看见她脸上因他的克制而飞快掠过的那抹失望。

===Original===
Keeping his eyes resolutely in front of him, he talked wearily and wearisomely of the meeting he had attended the night before, of how troublesome and irritating Mrs. Amos had been that day with her rheumatism, of his having at last persuaded Mrs. Hiram Tucker to go to Atlanta to have the operation she had so many times postponed. Jane answered him abstractedly and in monosyllables. At last she moved, almost with obvious meaning, to the canvas porch swing and there rested against the pillows piled in one corner. And yet Kenneth talked drearily on and on and on. He spoke at length of a conversation he had had with Bob that morning—of how glad he was that Bob was going away to school. Jane swung gently back and forth—and said nothing. Mr. Phillips came out on the porch and offered Kenneth a cigar, which he accepted and lighted. Mr. Phillips sat down and talked garrulously while the two men smoked. Jane felt that she could hardly keep from screaming. After what seemed an hour, Mr. Phillips, his topics of conversation exhausted, and at a sign from Jane that was not to be disregarded, rose heavily and lumbered into the house again.

Kenneth threw away the stump of his cigar. It had suddenly occurred to him that Jane hadn’t said very much for the past hour. He rose to go.

Jane sat silent as though unmindful of his having risen. He looked closely at her. Tears of he knew not what stood in her eyes. He dropped to the seat beside her, wondering what he had done to hurt her so. “Jane, what’s the matter?” he asked in a troubled voice. “What have I done?” She looked at him.… He didn’t know what happened next. Suddenly he found her in his arms. He strained her to him with all the passion he had been restraining for the months that seemed like years. He kissed her hair. He mumbled incoherently, yet with perfect understanding, to Jane, tender endearments. At length she raised her face from where it had been buried on his chest, gazed straight into his eyes. Their lips met in a long, clinging, rapturous kiss.…

===Chinese===
他目光死死盯着正前方，乏味又乏味地讲着他头天晚上参加的那场会，讲阿莫斯太太那天犯风湿时有多磨人、多气人，讲他终于说动了海勒姆·塔克太太去亚特兰大做她推了又推的那场手术。简心不在焉地答应着，一个字一个字地往外蹦。末了她挪动身子，那动作几乎是再明显不过地含着意思，挪到门廊上那张帆布吊椅那边，倚在堆于一角的靠枕上。可肯尼思还是枯燥地一个劲儿讲下去。他又讲到今早和鲍勃的一席谈——讲到自己多高兴鲍勃要去外地念书。简轻轻地荡来荡去——什么也不说。菲利普斯先生走到门廊上，递给肯尼思一支雪茄，他接过来点着了。菲利普斯先生坐下，两个男人一边抽烟他一边絮叨个不停。简觉得自己简直要尖叫出来才好。过了仿佛一个钟头，菲利普斯先生能聊的话题都聊尽了，又见简使了个不容忽视的眼色，这才吃力地站起来，拖着沉重的脚步又挪进屋里去了。

肯尼思把雪茄蒂一扔。他猛地意识到，过去这一个钟头简没说几句话。他站起来要走。

简默默坐着，仿佛没察觉他站起来了。他凑近看她。她眼里噙着一泡泪，他不知道那是为了什么。他一屁股坐到她身旁的位子上，纳闷自己究竟做了什么，竟伤她如此。「简，你怎么啦？」他忧心地问，「我做了什么？」她望着他……他不知道接下来发生了什么。忽然之间，他发现她已在自己的怀里。他用这几个月——漫长得像几年的几个月——一直压抑着的全部激情，把她紧紧搂向自己。他吻她的头发。他语无伦次地向简喃喃低语着温柔的情话，却彼此心领神会。末了，她把一直埋在他胸前的脸抬起来，直直地凝视他的眼睛。他们的唇相遇，印下一个悠长的、难分难舍的、销魂的吻……

===Original===
“How long have you known?” he asked her. Men are such idiots—they are never satisfied to take what comes to them—they must ask silly and nonsensical questions.

She told him. Of her long struggle, of her decision, of her annoyance at his blindness. They talked eagerly until long past the hour of ten. He heard Mr. Phillips moving chairs and dropping his shoes—obvious hints that the time to go had long since passed. They paid no attention to these danger signals but laughed softly to themselves.

Everything must end eventually. Kenneth walked homewards through the soft light of the September moon. Amusedly, the phrase “walking on air” occurred to him. He laughed aloud. “Walking on air” was as the rheumatic stumping along of old Mrs. Amos compared to the way he felt.…

===Chinese===
「你早就知道了吗？」他问她。男人真是些傻瓜——他们从不知足于到手的福气——非得问些又傻又没意思的问题。

她告诉了他。讲她那漫长的挣扎，她的决定，她对他的迟钝的恼火。他们热烈地谈着，一直谈到十点过后许久。他听见菲利普斯先生在挪椅子、在丢鞋子——这都是再明显不过的暗示：早该走了。他们对这些危险信号全不理会，只自顾自地轻声笑。

凡事总有尽头。肯尼思在九月柔和的月光里往家走。「如履云端」[6]这几个字，逗趣地浮上他心头。他出声笑了。「如履云端」拿来比方他此刻的感觉，那就好比拿患风湿的老阿莫斯太太一瘸一拐的挪步来比方一样……

---

> 译注：
>
> [1] **chlo’form（氯仿）**：原文 chlo’form 为 chloroform（氯仿，一种吸入式麻醉剂）的黑人乡土方言读法。塔克太太对麻醉的恐惧，反映了二十世纪初美国南方黑人对现代医学既向往又畏惧的心态。译文保留方言口吻作「氯仿（chlo’form）」。
>
> [2] **「黑镇」（Darktown）**：白人对黑人聚居区的蔑称，主表已收。本章鲍勃口中此词带反讽意味——三K党竟敢直接闯入黑人聚居区行凶。
>
> [3] **N.N.F.C.P.L.**：即第十二章成立的「全国黑人农户合作保护同盟（The National Negro Farmers’ Cooperative and Protective League）」之首字母缩写。本章肯尼思在全县铺开其分部。
>
> [4] ***esprit cordial*（由衷的亲睦）**：法语，意为「真诚的亲善、热忱」。原文以斜体标示此为外来语，译文保留斜体并附原文，加中译「由衷的亲睦」。怀特以此词反衬黑白之间旧日表面的和睦，在一夜暴行之后荡然无存。
>
> [5] **摩西（Moses）**：《圣经》中引领以色列人出埃及、走向应许之地的领袖（《出埃及记》）。此处黑人农户视肯尼思为引领他们摆脱经济奴役的新摩西，呼应全书「应许之地（promised land）」之喻。
>
> [6] **「如履云端」（walking on air）**：英语习语，形容欣喜若狂、飘飘欲仙。肯尼思以阿莫斯太太患风湿的蹒跚步态作戏谑反衬，极言自己求爱成功后轻飘飘的狂喜。

## XV / 十五

===Original===
It was the next night. In the gully on the road leading from that one out of Central City which went northward, there was being held a hastily called meeting of Central City Klan, Knights of the Ku Klux Klan, Realm of Georgia. Before, there had been three hundred robed figures. Tonight, three months later, the popularity of organized intolerance was attested to by the presence of fully five hundred. What had happened to Nancy Ware had acted as a powerful incentive to the recruiting of new converts. It was mighty fine to have a strong and powerful organization to shut mouths of those who talked too much about the nighttime deeds of loyal Klansmen. And, by gum, if you’re doing anything you don’t want known or stopped, you’d better be on the inside.

A figure whose arms waved excitedly as he talked was haranguing the crowd, which paid close attention to him. Had Tom Tracy been there, he would certainly have recognized the voice of the speaker. Ed Stewart’s wife, had she been there, would also have recognized it and dragged the speaker home by force had he resisted.

===Chinese===
那是次一夜。在中央城朝北那条路岔出去的一条沟壑里，中央城三K党——三K党骑士团佐治亚州分部——正召开一次临时召集的大会。上回，袍服裹身的人影有三百之数。三个月后的今夜，有组织的不宽容其风靡程度，由足足五百人的到场可见一斑。南希·韦尔遭逢的那桩事，成了招徕新信徒的有力引子。有这样一个强势有力的组织来封住那些嘴——专封那些对忠诚三K党徒（Klansmen）的夜间行迹多嘴多舌的人——真是太妙了。再说，天哪，你若正在做什么不愿叫人知晓、不愿叫人拦阻的事，那最好你自己就在这组织里头。

一个说话时手臂激动挥舞的人影，正在对人群慷慨陈词，众人凝神听着。汤姆·特雷西若在场，定能听出这说话人的嗓子。埃德·斯图尔特（Ed Stewart）[1]的太太若在场，也能听出来——他若不肯回家，便要硬把他拖回去。

===Original===
“White civilization in the South is tottering on its throne!” he shouted. “We who hold in our hands the future of civilization have been asleep! While we have gone about our ways, the damn niggers are plottin’ to kill us all in our beds! Right now they’re bringing into our fair city great passels of guns and ammunition marked ‘sewin’ m’chines’ and ‘ploughs’! They’re meetin’ ev’ry night in these nigger churches all over the county and they’re plottin’ an’ plannin’ to kill ev’ry white man, woman an’ chile in this county and take the lan’ for themselves! They’re led by a damn nigger doctor right here in Central City named Harper! I know it’s so, ’cause another nigger doctor named Williams tol’ me yestiddy mornin’ all about it and said that this nigger Harper was leadin’ this vile plot! He’s been goin’ all over the county stirrin’ up the damn niggers and incitin’ them to murder all of us! What’re you men goin’ to do?” he challenged in a voice that shrilled in pretended rage and terror.

A deep-throated roar answered him. Cries of “Kill the bastards!” “Lynch ’em!” “Kill every black bastard befo’ mornin!” It was the age-long voice of the mob bent on murder—the pack in full cry. But it was more than the voice of the mob of the Roman Colosseum, for that ancient cry was one of pleasure at the death of a single Christian. This was the shout of those intent on a wild, murderous rampage that spared neither man, woman, nor child.

===Chinese===
「南方的白人文明正从宝座上摇摇欲坠！」他吼道，「把文明的未来攥在手中的我们，竟一直在打瞌睡！我们各忙各事的时候，那帮该死的黑鬼正密谋着把我们一个个死在自家床上！这会儿他们正把一包包一捆捆枪支弹药运进我们这座好城，外头标着『缝纫机』、『犁』！他们每晚在县里各处的黑鬼教堂里碰头，阴谋策划，要把这县里每一个白人男女老少全杀了，把地全占归他们！领头的，就是中央城本地一个该死的黑鬼医生，姓哈珀！这事我千真万确，因为另一个姓威廉斯的黑鬼医生，昨天早上亲口把一切告诉了我，说就是这个黑鬼哈珀在牵头主使这桩阴毒的阴谋！他满县乱跑，煽动那帮该死的黑鬼，怂恿他们把我们全杀光！你们这些男人，打算怎么办？」他以一声尖锐的、佯装盛怒与惊恐的嗓音发难。

一声发自胸腔深处的怒吼回应了他。「杀掉这帮杂种！」「动私刑！」「天亮前杀光每一个黑杂种！」这是一心行凶的暴民那自古以来的嗓门——是猎犬群穷追时的嘶吠。可比罗马斗兽场那暴民的呐喊还要可怖：那古时的嘶吼，是一个基督徒死去时取乐的欢呼；这一片，却是一群打定主意要放手屠戮、不论男女老幼一概不饶的人的呐喊。

===Original===
“Klansmen!”

A voice like that of a bull roared until the tumult had subsided. It was the Exalted Cyclops of the Central City Klan. He stood in silence until the group of hooded figures was still.

“The noble order of the Ku Klux Klan don’t handle situations such as this like a mob!” The figures stood expectantly, eagerly waiting to hear what would come next.

“We have listened to the story told by our fellow Klansmen. Hol’ yo’se’ves ready for the call of the Invisible Empire at any minute. We have planned the way to en’ this dastardly plot and to punish those responsible with death!”

“That’s right! Kill ’em! Lynch ’em! Burn th’ bastards!” shouted the crowd.

“That’ll be done till ev’ry one is killed!” promised the Exalted Cyclops. “But it can’t be done so’s it can be laid to our noble order! Already our enemies are charging us with crimes! The Fed’ral Gov’nment will be down on our heads!”

===Chinese===
「三K党徒！」

一声如公牛般的吼叫，直到骚乱平息才止。是中央城三K党的崇高独眼巨人（Exalted Cyclops）[2]。他默然立着，等这一群罩头戴帽的人影静下来。

「三K党这一尊贵骑士团，处理这等情形，可不像一群暴民！」众袍影屏息期待，急切等着下文。

「我们已听过了同党的陈述。各位随时待命，听候无形帝国（Invisible Empire）[3]号令。我们已筹划好怎样了结这桩卑劣阴谋，并把其主谋者处以死罪！」

「对！杀！动私刑！烧死这帮杂种！」人群喊。

「这事要做到杀光每一个为止！」崇高独眼巨人许诺，「但不能做得让人能把账算到我们这尊贵骑士团头上！我们的敌人已在罗织我们罪名！联邦政府要降罪到我们头上的！」

===Original===
There were cries of “Damn the Gov’nment!” from some of the more hotheaded. But calmer judgment prevailed. Something was to be done, but what that ominous “something” might be, was not revealed. Each man was to be ready for instantaneous duty upon call of the Klan. Immediate action was not wise, for the Klan investigators had not completed their work. Action must wait until that had been done, for it was essential that not one of the plotters should escape.

This last point was emphasized. At last the crowd became more calm with the determination to postpone its vengeance until it was certain of being complete. It then dispersed its several ways, dissolving into separate groups that talked excitedly of the astounding and terrifying news, the need of prompt action, the great luck the white folks had had in discovering the plot so soon, violent denunciation of the Negroes in the plot.

In one of the groups the conversation was different. One of the group was the Exalted Cyclops, in private life Sheriff Bob Parker; another was the Kligrapp, otherwise Henry Lane, Commissioner of Health; the third was the speaker who had revealed the plot, Ed Stewart, Tom Tracy’s landlord.

Sheriff Parker chuckled softly. “Well, Ed, looks like somethin’ is about to break loose, eh?” he observed.

“Yep, I reck’n you’re right. Them damn niggers’ve got a hell of a nerve! Formin’ sassieties to ‘stop robbin’ sharecroppers’! When we get through with ’em, they’ll be formin’ coal-shov’lin’ sassieties in hell!” The other two joined in the laugh at his grim joke. “We’ll put in th’ papers they was formin’ to kill white folks and they’ll never know but what that ain’t true.”

Parker laughed again. Waving his hand at the departing Klansmen, there came to his face a cynical sneer. “An’ them damn fools really think they’re sho’ly goin’ to be murdered by the damn niggers!”

===Chinese===
几个更头脑发热的人喊出「去他妈的政府！」。但冷静一派的判断占了上风。是要动手，可那不详的「动手」究竟为何，并未挑明。每人都得随时待命，一俟三K党召唤便立即当差。立即动手不智，因为党内的调查人员尚未完工。行动得等那一桩完成，因为绝不能让一个阴谋者漏网，这至关重要。

最后这一点被反复强调。人群总算更平静了些，决意将其报复推迟到确有把握做绝之时。随即四散，化为三五成群，兴奋地议论这骇人听闻的消息、及早动手的必要、白人如此之快便识破阴谋的鸿运、以及对阴谋中黑人的恶毒咒骂。

其中一群人的对话却不同。群中一人是那位崇高独眼巨人，在日常生活里是鲍勃·帕克警长（Sheriff Bob Parker）；另一人是纪录官（Kligrapp）[4]，即卫生委员亨利·莱恩；第三人便是揭露阴谋的发言者，埃德·斯图尔特，汤姆·特雷西的东家。

帕克警长轻声嘿嘿一笑。「哎，埃德，看来要有事要爆开啦，嗯？」他说。

「可不，我估摸你说得对。那帮该死的黑鬼胆子也太肥了！居然结社『要制止抢掠佃农』！等我们收拾完他们，他们就要到地狱里去组什么铲煤社了！」另两人跟着他这句阴狠的笑话一齐笑起来。「我们在报上给他们栽一条——说他们是要杀白人，他们一辈子也不知道这不是真的。」

帕克又笑了。他朝散去的三K党徒一挥手，脸上浮起一丝讥讽的冷笑。「那帮该死的傻瓜，还真以为自己铁定要被那帮该死的黑鬼谋杀了呢！」

===Original===
---

In another section of Central City there was being enacted at the same time another scene of poignant drama that threatened to translate itself into tragedy. The place was a darkened bedroom in the home of Roy Ewing on Georgia Avenue, and the actors in it were four in number. Roy Ewing, owner and manager of the Ewing General Merchandise Store, whom Kenneth had seen but little since Ewing had discontinued his nocturnal visits to Kenneth’s office, was one of the actors. His wife, whose face still bore evidences of a youthful beauty that was fast fading, was a second. A third was old Dr. Bennett, who sat by the bed, his hair dishevelled, his face lined with perplexity and anxiety, as he apprehensively watched the fourth actor in the drama, a girl of nineteen who was restlessly tossing in pain on the bed. Row Ewing stood at the foot of the bed. His wife sat on the other side uttering little snatches of phrases of soothing sympathy which her daughter did not hear.

===Chinese===
---

在中央城的另一头，同一时刻正上演着另一幕揪心的人间戏剧，眼看就要转成惨剧。地点是乔治亚大道（Georgia Avenue）罗伊·尤因家一间昏暗的卧房，戏中人共四位。罗伊·尤因——尤因杂货百货店的老板兼掌柜，自他不再夜里往肯尼思诊所跑之后，肯尼思很少见到他——是其中一位。他的妻子是第二位，脸上仍存几分正飞快褪去的年轻时的姿色。第三位是老贝内特医生，他坐在床边，头发凌乱，脸上因困顿与焦急而布满皱纹，忧心忡忡地盯着这场戏的第四位主角——一个十九岁的姑娘，正在床上痛得辗转翻滚。罗伊·尤因站在床尾。他妻子坐在另一边，断断续续地说着几句抚慰怜惜的话，可她女儿一句也没听见。

===Original===
Dr. Bennett was plainly worried and at a loss what to do to relieve the torture Ewing’s daughter was so clearly experiencing. He turned to Ewing. “Roy, to tell you the truth, it don’t seem like I can find out what’s the matter with Mary. When she had that first attack, I thought she had appendicitis, but she ain’t got no fever to speak of, so it can’t be her appendix that’s botherin’ her. Looks like t’ me she’s got some sort of bleedin’ inside, but I can’t tell.”

Ewing and his wife looked anxiously first at their daughter, then interrogatively and pleadingly at the old physician as he watched the sufferer in her contortions of pain and agony. Mary, married two months and her husband working in Atlanta, had lived with her parents after a short honeymoon. She had her mother’s beauty—that is, the delicate, patrician, statuesque charm that had been her mother’s when Roy Ewing had courted and won her two decades ago in Charleston, South Carolina. It was not the harsh-lined, blonde beauty of Georgia but the fragile old-world, French loveliness of that spot in South Carolina where French tradition and customs and features had not yet been barbarized by the infusion of that Anglo-Saxon blood which is the boast of the South. She lay there, a pitiful sight. Her face was pale, covered with cold, clammy perspiration; all blood had fled from it. She breathed with great difficulty in short and laboured respiratory efforts. Her pulse was failing, very rapid and thready; at times it was barely perceptible. She had been seized with the attack around seven o’clock, when she began vomiting. Now she appeared to be so weakened with the pain she had endured that a state of coma was obviously fast approaching. At least it seemed so. Dr. Bennett tried to revive her, but with little success. The absence of fever puzzled him. He feared an internal haemorrhage—all signs pointed to such a condition—yet he did not know. Roy Ewing and his wife were among his closest friends. He would have tried an operation had they not been. That he feared to risk with their daughter. Yet, what could he do? Mary was obviously so weak that he knew she could not be moved to Atlanta, three hundred miles away. Nor would a physician be able to get to Central City in time to operate.

===Chinese===
贝内特医生显然心焦，又束手无策，不知如何纾解尤因女儿显然正在承受的苦痛。他转向尤因。「罗伊，跟你说实话，我好像就是查不出玛丽到底怎么了。她头一回发病那阵，我当是阑尾炎，可她又没什么热度好说，所以闹她的不可能是阑尾。我看着倒像是她体内有什么出血，可我又说准不了。」

尤因和他妻子焦急地一会儿看看女儿，一会儿用探询的、恳求的目光看看这位老医生——后者正盯着这位受苦的人在痛楚的扭动里挣扎。玛丽结婚两个月，丈夫在亚特兰大做事，蜜月过后便回了娘家与父母同住。她生得有母亲的那份美——也就是二十年前罗伊·尤因在南卡罗来纳州查尔斯顿（Charleston, South Carolina）追求并娶她母亲时，她母亲所有的那种纤秀、贵族气、雕像般的韵致。那不是佐治亚那种线条硬朗的金发美，而是南卡那块地方那种旧世界的、法兰西式的柔弱可人——那里的法国传统、习俗与相貌，尚未被那为南方所自夸的盎格鲁-撒克逊血统所注入、所蛮化。她躺在那里，景象可怜。脸白得没有一丝血色，盖着一层冷而黏的汗。她呼吸艰难，一下一下短而费力。她的脉在衰败，跳得极快、细如游丝；有时几乎摸不着。她约莫七点钟光景发作起来，开始呕吐。此刻她似乎已被所受的疼痛耗得如此虚弱，昏迷眼看就要来了。至少看着是这样。贝内特医生试图唤醒她，收效甚微。没有发热这一点让他困惑。他怕是内出血——种种迹象都指向那一桩——可他又拿不准。罗伊·尤因夫妇是他最亲近的朋友。换了别人，他早就试手术了。他们的女儿，他不敢冒险。可他又能怎样？玛丽显然虚弱得经不起挪到三百英里外的亚特兰大。也来不及请别处的医生赶到中央城动手术。

===Original===
“I’m puzzled, Roy, mighty puzzled,” he said, turning to Ewing. “I might as well tell you the truth. Looks like t’ me she c’n hardly last till mornin’.” It was gall and wormwood for him to admit his impotency, but he did it.

“Dr. Bennett, you’ve got to do somethin’! You’ve got to! You’ve got to!”

It was Mrs. Ewing who cried out in her agony—the piteous cry of a mother who sees her firstborn dying before her eyes. Her face was as blanched as Mary’s—every drop of blood seemed to have been drained from it. She looked pleadingly at him, chill terror gripping her heart as she realized from his words that her Mary, who had been so happy and well that morning, was about to die.

“If you—all wasn’t such good friends of mine, I’d try it anyhow,” Dr. Bennett answered her, his voice as agonized as hers. “But I’m skeered to op’rate or do anythin’ that might hasten her on.”

Ewing walked over to the doctor, grasped the older man’s shoulders so fiercely that he winced in pain.

“By God,” he shouted at Dr. Bennett, “you’ve got to operate! I can’t see my little Mary die right here befo’ my eyes! Go ahead and do what you think best. It’ll be better’n seein’ her die while we stand here doin’ nothin’!”.

===Chinese===
「我犯难了，罗伊，太犯难了，」他转向尤因说，「我干脆跟你说实话吧。我看她，难得挨过今晚到天亮。」承认自己无能为力，于他如饮胆吞黄连，可他还是说了。

「贝内特医生，你总得想点办法！总得想！总得想！」

喊出来的是尤因太太，她在剧痛中呼号——那是一个母亲眼睁睁看着头生女儿死在面前的凄惨呼喊。她的脸白得与玛丽一般无二——仿佛每一滴血都从脸上抽干了。她以恳求的目光望着他，一股冰冷的恐惧攥住她的心：她从他的话里明白过来，她那个今早还好好端端、开开心心的玛丽，就要死了。

「你一家——要不是跟我交情这么深，我横竖也要试一试，」贝内特医生答她，话音与她一般痛苦，「可我害怕动刀，怕做任何可能催她上路的事。」

尤因走到医生跟前，凶狠地抓住老人的双肩，抓得老人疼得直皱眉。

「天哪，」他对贝内特医生吼，「你得动手术！我不能眼睁睁看着我的小玛丽死在我面前！你去吧，照你以为最好的去做。总比我们站在这儿干看着她死强！」。

===Original===
“Roy,” Dr. Bennett groaned, “you know there ain’t anythin’ I wouldn’t do for you—’cept this.” He waved his hand vaguely towards the bed. As he did so, he looked with keen appraisement at Ewing in the dim light. He seemed to be debating in his mind whether or not he dared take a very long chance. If the chance would not be more disastrous. If Mary’s life might not be better lost than that! Ewing almost stopped breathing as he saw the momentary indecision in the physician’s face. Mrs. Ewing saw none of this byplay, for she had sunk down on the bed, where her body was shaken with the sobs she could not restrain.

“There’s jus’ one chance t’ save her,” Dr. Bennett hesitatingly began. Ewing leaned forward in his eagerness.

“There’s jus’ this one hope,” Dr. Bennett repeated, “but I don’t know if you’d be willin’ to take that chance.”

“I don’t give a damn what it is!” Ewing shouted in his anxiety. “I’ll take it! What is it, Doc? I don’t care what it costs! What is it?” He quivered as with a chill in his excitement—the excitement of the drowning man who sees a possible rescuer as he is about to go down for the third time. Mrs. Ewing had stopped crying—she seemed as though she had forgotten to breathe. They both waited eagerly for the older man to speak. At last he did. He paused after each word.

“Th’only—man—I know—near enough—to op’rate—in time—is—a—nigger-doctor—here—named—Harper!”

===Chinese===
「罗伊，」贝内特医生呻吟，「你知道我没有什么不肯为你做的——除了这一桩。」他朝床那边含糊地一挥手。他这样挥着，在昏暗中以锐利的目光打量尤因。他似乎在心里盘算，自己敢不敢赌一手——赌一个万分之一的可能。万一这赌注更糟呢。万一玛丽的命，丢得还不如那样丢了好呢！尤因一见医生脸上那一瞬的迟疑，几乎屏住了呼吸。尤因太太对这些暗中的波折一无所见，她已瘫倒在床上，身子被忍不住的抽泣抖得直颤。

「只有——一个法子——能救她，」贝内特医生迟疑着开口。尤因急切地身子前倾。

「只有这一线指望，」贝内特医生重复，「可我不知道你肯不肯冒这个险。」

「管他是什么！」尤因焦急地喊，「我冒！是什么，医生？我不在乎代价！是什么？」他激动得像打寒噤一样发抖——那是溺水的人将要第三次沉下去时，望见一个可能的救星时的激动。尤因太太已止了哭——她仿佛忘了呼吸。两人都急切地等老人开口。他终于开了口。一字一字，每字之后一顿。

「这附近——唯一——来得及——动这个——手术的——人——我认识的——是——本地——一个——黑鬼医生——姓——哈珀！」

===Original===
“Oh, my God!” groaned Ewing as he sank to his knees beside the bed and buried his face in his hands. “A nigger—seein’ my Mary—operatin’ on her—Good God! I’d rather see her dead than have a nigger put his hands on her! No! No! No!” He fairly screamed the last in his fury.

“I didn’t think you’d do it,” said Dr. Bennett miserably. “I jus’ felt I oughter tell you. He’s jus’ out of school—studied in one of the bes’ schools up No’th—and in France. He might save Mary—but I can’t blame you none for not havin’ him.”

While he was speaking, Ewing jumped to his feet and paced up and down the room like a caged and wounded tiger. On the one hand was the life of his daughter—on the other his inherent, acquired, environmental prejudice. None but those who know intimately the depth and passion of that prejudice as it flourishes in the South can know what torture what a hell—what agony Ewing was going through. Prejudice under almost any circumstances is hard enough to bear—in Ewing’s case his very soul was tormented at such an unheard-of thing as a Negro operating on his daughter.

===Chinese===
「哦，天哪！」尤因呻吟一声，跪倒在床边，把脸埋进双手里。「一个黑鬼——看我玛丽——给她动刀——老天爷！我宁可看着她死，也不让一个黑鬼碰她！不！不！不！」末了几句他简直是在狂怒中嘶喊。

「我本就知道你不肯，」贝内特医生痛苦地说，「我只是觉得该告诉你。他刚出校门——在北方一所顶好的学校念过书——还在法国进修过。他或许能救玛丽——可你不肯用他，我也不能怪你。」

他说话的工夫，尤因已一跃而起，在屋里来回踱步，像一只关在笼里、受了伤的老虎。一头是他女儿的命——一头是他生来的、习得的、环境浸润的那份偏见。除非深切了解南方那种偏见的根深与酷烈，无人能知尤因此刻受的是怎样的折磨、怎样的地狱、怎样的煎熬。偏见在几乎任何情形下都够难熬——而在尤因，一个黑人动他女儿的刀，这种闻所未闻的事，直折磨到了他的灵魂深处。

===Original===
“Roy!”

He turned abruptly at the sound of his wife’s voice, having forgotten for the time everything—wife, surroundings, all—as he struggled with the problem he faced.

“Roy!” Her voice was weak because of the ordeal through which she was passing. She ran to him, seizing his arm and looking up at him pleadingly.

“Roy! I can’t see our Mary die! I can’t let her die!”

“Would you have a nigger see her naked?” he demanded of her fiercely. “Would you? Would you?”

Her head went back sharply at the roughness of his tone. In her eyes flashed that brilliant, burning look of mother love that submits to no dangers, no obstacles.

“I’d do anything to save her!” she cried.

“No, no, Mary,” Ewing pleaded, “we can’t do that! We can’t!”

She did not hear him. Brushing past him, she caught Dr. Bennett by the arm as he rose to his feet. “Get that doctor here quick!” she demanded of him.…

===Chinese===
「罗伊！」

他蓦地转身——他妻子叫他。方才他与这道难题搏斗，一时把什么都忘了——妻子、周遭、一切。

「罗伊！」她声音虚弱，因她正经历的熬炼。她跑到他跟前，抓住他的胳膊，仰头以恳求的目光望着他。

「罗伊！我不能眼看着我们的玛丽死！我不能让她死！」

「你要让一个黑鬼看她赤身裸体？」他凶狠地反诘她，「你肯？你肯？」

他那粗暴的语调让她猛地把头一仰。她眼里闪出那种辉煌的、炽烈的、不避任何险阻艰危的母爱的光。

「只要能救她，我什么都肯！」她喊。

「不，不，玛丽，」尤因哀求，「我们不能那样！不能！」

她没听见他的话。她擦过他身侧，在贝内特医生起身时一把抓住老人的胳膊。「快把那个医生请来！」她向他命令。……

===Original===
When Dr. Bennett telephoned him to come to Roy Ewing’s home as quickly as he could, Kenneth was somewhat puzzled. He went at once, deciding that one of the servants was sick. When told that it was Mary Ewing he was to treat, he could not conceal his amazement. He followed Roy Ewing and the doctor to her room, the while he was trying to make himself realize that he, Kenneth Harper, a Negro doctor, had been called to treat a white person—a white woman—in the South. Reaching the bedside, though, he put aside his bewilderment and began at once the diagnosis to discover what the trouble was. He listened without speaking to Dr. Bennett as the old man told him the symptoms Mary had shown and what he thought was the matter. Ewing was sent from the room. Kenneth rapidly examined the patient—and decided that she was having severe internal haemorrhages. It looked like an acute and dangerous case.

Immediate operation seemed the only hope. And even that hope was a slim one. He informed Dr. Bennett of his diagnosis.

Ewing was summoned. Briefly Kenneth told him his theory of the trouble—that the only hope was immediate operation. Ewing faltered, hesitated, seemed about to refuse to allow it. At that moment a loud scream of pain was wrung from Mary’s lips. He winced as though he had been struck. He shrugged his shoulders in assent to the operation.…

===Chinese===
贝内特医生打电话叫他尽快到罗伊·尤因家去，肯尼思有些纳闷。他立刻动身，揣测是哪个佣人生了病。等知道要他治的是玛丽·尤因，他掩饰不住自己的惊愕。他跟着罗伊·尤因和医生进她的卧房，一路还在努力让自己相信：他，肯尼思·哈珀，一个黑人医生，竟在南方被请来治一个白人——一个白人女人。可一到床边，他便把这份惶惑撇开，立刻着手诊断，探究病因。他一言不发地听贝内特医生讲玛丽表现的病症、以及他的判断。尤因被打发出房。肯尼思迅速检查病人——断定她正在大量内出血。这是急症、险症。

立即手术似乎是唯一的指望。而这指望也渺茫。他把诊断告诉贝内特医生。

尤因被叫回。肯尼思简短地把病因判断告诉他——唯一的希望是立即手术。尤因口吃起来，犹豫着，似乎要拒绝。那一刻，玛丽嘴里迸出一声剧痛的尖叫。他像挨了一击似的身子一缩。他耸了耸肩，表示应允手术。……

===Original===
Kenneth telephoned Mrs. Johnson, the nurse who had helped him before, to be ready to go with him for an operation in ten minutes. He drove rapidly home, secured his instruments, ether, sterilizer, gown and other equipment, bundled them into his car, called for Mrs. Johnson, explaining briefly to her the nature of the case as he drove as rapidly as he could to the Ewing home.

Mary was carried downstairs and placed on the dining-room table. Dr. Bennett agreed to give the anaesthesic. Kenneth went rapidly, yet surely, to work. In his element now, he forgot time, place, the unusual circumstances, and everything else. Swiftly he began the delicate and perilous task as soon as Dr. Bennett had sufficiently etherized the patient. Yet, even in the stress of the moment, he could not keep down the ironical thoughts that crept to his brain in spite of all efforts to bar them. The South’s a funny place, he mused. Must have been a mighty hard thing for old Bennett to have to admit that he, a Negro, knew more about operating in a case like this than he did himself. Roy Ewing must have had a bad half-hour deciding whether or not he’d let a Negro do the operation on his daughter. Hope nothing goes wrong—if it does, might as well pick out some other town to go to. Oh, well, won’t let that worry me. Have to make the best of it—save her if possible.

===Chinese===
肯尼思给约翰逊太太——以前帮过他的那位护士——打电话，要她十分钟后随他出诊手术。他飞车回家，取了器械、乙醚、消毒器、手术衣及其他装备，一股脑塞进车里，接上约翰逊太太，一边尽力把车开快往尤因家赶，一边简短地向她交代了病情。

玛丽被抬下楼，安放在餐桌上。贝内特医生答应施麻醉。肯尼思下手快而稳。此刻他如鱼得水，忘了时间、地点、处境的奇异，忘了一切。贝内特医生一给病人充分麻醉，他便立刻着手这精细而凶险的活计。然而纵在此时此刻的紧要关头，他仍挡不住那些讽刺的念头偷偷爬进脑子——他怎样费心去堵也无济于事。南方这地方真怪，他暗想。老贝内特不得不承认，他这么个黑人在这一类手术上比他自己还懂行，这事想来真够他受。罗伊·尤因在决定让不让他一个黑人动他女儿的刀这件事上，必是熬了够难堪的半个钟头。但愿别出岔子——若出了岔子，还是趁早挑个别的镇子搬家为妙。哦，罢了，犯不着为这个操心。尽力而为——若可能，便救她。

===Original===
Weakened by the severe haemorrhages she had been having, Mary was in a condition of extreme shock. The least slip, Kenneth realized, and nothing could save her. Her face wan and drawn, Mary’s life hung precariously in the balance—the odds were all against her while the grim spectre of death crept slowly but surely upon her.

Beads of perspiration stood upon Kenneth’s brow as he fought for her life. Though he could not have done the operation himself, Dr. Bennett sensed the gravity of the situation. The older man leaned forward in his anxiety—hardly daring to breathe for fear of interrupting the deft, sure touch of the operator. Ten—fifteen—twenty—thirty—forty—fifty minutes crept by on lagging feet—to the two doctors and the nurse each minute seemed an hour.

===Chinese===
玛丽因连番大量失血，已陷入极度的休克。肯尼思明白，稍有差池，便无可挽回。她脸色惨白、形容枯槁，性命悬于一线——情势全然对她不利，而那狰狞的死神正缓缓地、却稳稳地向她逼近。

肯尼思为她搏命，额上沁出一粒粒汗珠。贝内特医生虽不能亲自主刀，也察觉到情势的凶险。老人焦灼地前倾着身子——几乎不敢喘气，唯恐打扰了主刀人那灵巧、笃定的手下。十分钟——十五分——二十分——三十分——四十分——五十分，拖着迟滞的步子挨过去——对两位医生和护士，每一分钟都像一个钟头。

===Original===
Despite all his efforts, Kenneth knew Mary was rapidly sinking. The loss of blood and strength, the severity of the shock, the enervating spasms of pain she had suffered, had sapped her strength until all resistive power was gone. Kenneth knew that Dr. Bennett knew this too—even in the desperate struggle he wondered what the other would say and do—if the girl died.
